\{\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N}
, i.e., a function
{\mathcal{Q}}\colon\prod_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
of the form

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}\left(g_{1},\dots,g_{N}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}s_{i,j}\left\langle g_{i},g_{j}\right\rangle=\mathrm{Trace}\left({\mathbf{G}}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{S}}_{N}{\mathbf{G}}\right)

where
{\mathbf{G}}=\begin{bmatrix}g_{1}&\cdots&g_{N}\end{bmatrix},{\mathbf{S}}_{N}=\left(s_{i,j}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
.
Here
s_{i,j}=s_{i,j}\left(H,\{u_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N-1},\tau_{U}\right)
has a hidden dependency on the entries of H-matrix,
u_{j}
’s and
\tau_{U}
.
If
{\mathcal{Q}}(g_{1},\dots,g_{N})\geq 0
for any
g_{1},\dots,g_{N}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
(which is equivalent to
{\mathbf{S}}_{N}\succeq 0
) then we informally say
{\mathcal{Q}}\geq 0\iff
(
C1
).
If that is the case, we can establish

\displaystyle\tau_{U}\left\|{\mathbb{A}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}+\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},x_{N}-y_{0}\right\rangle\leq U_{N}\leq\dots\leq U_{1}=0.

By Lemma
3.2
, this implies
\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{\left\|y_{0}-x_{\star}\right\|^{2}}{\tau_{U}^{2}}
.

OHM
is an example where this holds; selecting
\tau_{U}=N
and
u_{j}=\frac{j(j+1)}{N}
for
j=1,\dots,N-1
ensures (
C1
), leading to the final convergence rate
\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{\left\|y_{0}-x_{\star}\right\|^{2}}{N^{2}}
.
We refer to this proof strategy as
primal Lyapunov proof
.

Dual Lyapunov structure.

Consider a convergence proof for (
34
) with respect to the same performance measure
\left\|y_{N-1}-{\mathbb{T}}y_{N-1}\right\|^{2}=4\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}
, but now using a positive sequence
\{v_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N-1}
and
\{V_{j}\}_{j=0}^{N-1}
, defined by
V_{N-1}=0
and the backward recursion

\displaystyle V_{j}=V_{j+1}+v_{j+1}\left\langle x_{N}-x_{j+1},\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{j+1}\right\rangle

for
j=0,\dots,N-2
(note that we are using a different set of inequalities than
U_{j}
).
Then
\{V_{j}\}_{j=0}^{N-1}
is nonincreasing because
{\mathbb{A}}
is monotone.
As before,
\{v_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N-1}
can be seen as free variables.
Assume that we can show that for some
\tau_{V}>0
,

\displaystyle V_{0}\leq-\tau_{V}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}-\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},x_{N}-y_{0}\right\rangle

(C2)

holds
(in the sense of vector quadratic form with coefficients on
H
,
v_{j}
’s and
\tau_{V}
).
Then

\displaystyle 0=V_{N-1}\leq\dots\leq V_{0}\leq-\tau_{V}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}-\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},x_{N}-y_{0}\right\rangle.

By Lemma
3.2
, this implies
\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{\left\|y_{0}-x_{\star}\right\|^{2}}{\tau_{V}^{2}}
.

Dual-OHM
is an example satisfying this; selecting
\tau_{V}=N
and
v_{j}=\frac{N}{(N-j)(N-j+1)}
for
j=1,\dots,N-1
ensures (
C2
), which implies
\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{\left\|y_{0}-x_{\star}\right\|^{2}}{N^{2}}
.
We refer to this proof strategy as
dual Lyapunov proof
.

Finally, we are ready to state the H-duality theorem,
which shows that the primal Lyapunov proof for a “primal” algorithm can be transformed into a dual Lyapunov proof establishing the same rate for its H-dual algorithm.

Theorem D.1

(H-duality)
.

Consider sequences of positive real numbers
\{u_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N-1}
and
\{v_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N-1}
related through
v_{j}=\frac{1}{u_{N-j}}
for
j=1,\dots,N-1
.
Let
H\in\mathbb{R}^{(N-1)\times(N-1)}
be a lower triangular matrix and
\tau>0
. Then

\displaystyle\left[\text{\eqref{eqn::primal_cond_FPI} is satisfied with $H,\{u_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N-1}$ and $\tau_{U}=\tau$}\right]\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\left[\text{\eqref{eqn::dual_cond_FPI} is satisfied with $H^{\text{A}},\{v_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N-1}$ and $\tau_{V}=\tau$}\right].

D.2
Proof of Theorem
D.1

Define the two vector quadratic forms
{\mathcal{S}}
and
{\mathcal{T}}
by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1},\dots,\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N})

\displaystyle=U_{N}\left(H,\{u_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N-1}\right)-\tau\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}-\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},x_{N}\left(H\right)-y_{0}\right\rangle

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1},\dots,\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N})

\displaystyle=-V_{0}\left(H^{\text{A}},\{v_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N-1}\right)-\tau\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}-\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},x_{N}\left(H^{\text{A}}\right)-y_{0}\right\rangle,

where we write
U_{N}\left(H,\{u_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N-1}\right)
and
x_{N}(H)
to specify that these quantities are defined through the rules of
Section

D.1
while using the H-matrix
H
to define
x_{1},\dots,x_{N}
(according to (
34
)) and using those
x_{1},\dots,x_{N}
and the specified sequence
\{u_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N-1}
to define
U_{N}
.
Similarly,
V_{0}\left(H^{\text{A}},\{v_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N-1}\right)
and
x_{N}\left(H^{\text{A}}\right)
specifies that they are defined using
H^{\text{A}}
as H-matrix and using the sequence
\{v_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N-1}
.

Rewriting
{\mathcal{S}}
.

We first expand
{\mathcal{S}}
without explicitly expressing its dependency on
H
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1},\dots,\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N})

\displaystyle=U_{N}-\tau\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}-\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},x_{N}-y_{0}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}u_{k}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+1}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k},x_{k+1}-x_{k}\right\rangle-\tau\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}-\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},x_{N}-y_{0}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\tau\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}-\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},y_{N-1}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-y_{0}\right\rangle-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}u_{k}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+1}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k},y_{k}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+1}-y_{k-1}+\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-(\tau-1)\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}+\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},y_{0}-y_{N-1}\right\rangle+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}u_{k}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+1}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}\right\|^{2}-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}u_{k}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+1}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k},y_{k}-y_{k-1}\right\rangle

(35)

Now rewrite
\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},y_{0}-y_{N-1}\right\rangle=-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},y_{k}-y_{k-1}\right\rangle
and group this summation with the last summation within (
35
) to obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1},\dots,\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N})

\displaystyle=-(\tau-1)\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}u_{k}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+1}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}\right\|^{2}-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\left\langle u_{k}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+1}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k})+\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},y_{k}-y_{k-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-(\tau-1)\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}u_{k}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+1}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}\right\|^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\left\langle u_{k}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+1}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k})+\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}2h_{k,j+1}\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{j+1}\right\rangle

(36)

where in the last line we substitute
y_{k}-y_{k-1}
using the update rule (
34
) (which finally reveals the
H
-dependency of
{\mathcal{S}}
).

Rewriting
{\mathcal{T}}
.

We similarly expand
{\mathcal{T}}
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1},\dots,\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N})

\displaystyle=-V_{0}-\tau\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}-\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},x_{N}-y_{0}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}v_{k}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k},x_{N}-x_{k}\right\rangle-\tau\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}-\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},x_{N}-y_{0}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\tau\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}-\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},y_{N-1}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-y_{0}\right\rangle-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}v_{k}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k},y_{N-1}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-y_{k-1}+\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-(\tau-1)\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}+\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},y_{0}-y_{N-1}\right\rangle+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}v_{k}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}\right\|^{2}-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}v_{k}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k},y_{N-1}-y_{k-1}\right\rangle

(37)

We can rewrite the last summation in (
37
) as

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}v_{k}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k},y_{N-1}-y_{k-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\sum_{j=k}^{N-1}\left\langle v_{k}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}),y_{j}-y_{j-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{N-1}\sum_{k=1}^{j}\left\langle v_{k}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}),y_{j}-y_{j-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left\langle v_{j}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}),y_{k}-y_{k-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\left\langle v_{1}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1})+\cdots+v_{k}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}),y_{k}-y_{k-1}\right\rangle

where in the second-last equality, we switch the roles of indices
k
and
j
.
Now plug this back into (
37
) together with the identity
\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},y_{0}-y_{N-1}\right\rangle=-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},y_{k}-y_{k-1}\right\rangle
to obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1},\dots,\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N})

\displaystyle=-(\tau-1)\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}v_{k}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}+v_{1}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1})+\dots+v_{k}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}),y_{k}-y_{k-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-(\tau-1)\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}v_{k}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}+v_{1}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1})+\dots+v_{k}(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}),\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}2h_{N-j-1,N-k}\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{j+1}\right\rangle

(38)

where in the last line we substitute
y_{k}-y_{k-1}
using the update rule (
34
) but with entries of
H^{\text{A}}
, i.e.,
h_{N-j-1,N-k-1}
in place of
h_{k+1,j+1}
, which reveals the
H^{\text{A}}
-dependency of
{\mathcal{T}}
.

Equivalence of
{\mathcal{S}}\geq 0
and
{\mathcal{T}}\geq 0
.

Now we construct a one-to-one correspondence
\mathcal{F}\colon\prod_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\prod_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfying
{\mathcal{S}}(g_{1},\dots,g_{N})={\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{F}(g_{1},\dots,g_{N}))
.
Note that once this is established, it immediately follows that

\displaystyle\left[{\mathcal{S}}(g_{1},\dots,g_{N})\geq 0,\quad\forall\,g_{1},\dots,g_{N}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\right]\quad\iff\quad\left[{\mathcal{T}}(g_{1},\dots,g_{N})\geq 0,\quad\forall\,g_{1},\dots,g_{N}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\right].

Specifically, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left(g_{1},\dots,g_{N}\right)=\big(u_{N-1}(g_{N}-g_{N-1})+g_{N},\dots,u_{1}(g_{2}-g_{1})+g_{N},g_{N}\big)=(g_{1}^{\prime},\dots,g_{N}^{\prime}).

Then
{\mathcal{F}}
is a bijection because it has the explicit inverse mapping

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}(g_{1}^{\prime},\dots,g_{N}^{\prime})=\left(g_{N}^{\prime}+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\frac{1}{u_{N-k}}(g_{N}^{\prime}-g_{k}^{\prime}),\dots,g_{N}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{u_{N-1}}(g_{N}^{\prime}-g_{1}^{\prime}),g_{N}^{\prime}\right).

It remains to verify that
{\mathcal{S}}(g_{1},\dots,g_{N})={\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{F}(g_{1},\dots,g_{N}))
indeed holds true.
We directly plug in the transformed set of vectors
g_{1}^{\prime},\dots,g_{N}^{\prime}
to the expansion (
38
) of
{\mathcal{T}}
, and substitute
v_{k}=\frac{1}{u_{N-k}}
for
k=1,\dots,N
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}(g_{1}^{\prime},\dots,g_{N}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=-(\tau-1)\left\|g_{N}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}v_{k}\left\|g_{N}^{\prime}-g_{k}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\left\langle g_{N}^{\prime}+v_{1}(g_{N}^{\prime}-g_{1}^{\prime})+\dots+v_{k}(g_{N}^{\prime}-g_{k}^{\prime}),\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}2h_{N-j-1,N-k}g_{j+1}^{\prime}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\begin{split}&=-(\tau-1)\left\|g_{N}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\frac{1}{u_{N-k}}\left\|g_{N}^{\prime}-g_{k}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\left\langle g_{N}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{u_{N-1}}(g_{N}^{\prime}-g_{1}^{\prime})+\dots+\frac{1}{u_{N-k}}(g_{N}^{\prime}-g_{k}^{\prime}),\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}2h_{N-j-1,N-k}g_{j+1}^{\prime}\right\rangle\end{split}

(39)

Note that for
k=1,\dots,N-1
, it follows directly from the definition of
{\mathcal{F}}
that
g_{N}^{\prime}-g_{k}^{\prime}=u_{N-k}(g_{N-k}-g_{N-k+1})
.
Therefore, the first two terms of (
39
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle-(\tau-1)\left\|g_{N}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\frac{1}{u_{N-k}}\left\|g_{N}^{\prime}-g_{k}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=-(\tau-1)\left\|g_{N}\right\|^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}u_{N-k}\left\|g_{N-k}-g_{N-k+1}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=-(\tau-1)\left\|g_{N}\right\|^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}u_{k}\left\|g_{k+1}-g_{k}\right\|^{2}

and the last expression coincides with the first two terms within (
36
).
Next, rewrite the second line of (
39
) as following:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\left\langle g_{N}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{u_{N-1}}(g_{N}^{\prime}-g_{1}^{\prime})+\dots+\frac{1}{u_{N-k}}(g_{N}^{\prime}-g_{k}^{\prime}),\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}2h_{N-j-1,N-k}g_{j+1}^{\prime}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\left\langle g_{N}+(g_{N-1}-g_{N})+\dots+(g_{N-k}-g_{N-k+1}),\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}2h_{N-j-1,N-k}g_{j+1}^{\prime}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\left\langle g_{N-k},\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}2h_{N-j-1,N-k}g_{j+1}^{\prime}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}2h_{N-j-1,N-k}\left\langle g_{N-k},g_{N}+u_{N-j-1}(g_{N-j}-g_{N-j-1})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\sum_{i=N-k}^{N-1}2h_{i,N-k}\left\langle g_{N-k},g_{N}+u_{i}(g_{i+1}-g_{i})\right\rangle

where to obtain the last equality, we make an index substitution
i=N-j-1
.
From the last line, make another substitution of index
\ell=N-k
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\sum_{i=N-k}^{N-1}2h_{i,N-k}\left\langle g_{N-k},g_{N}+u_{i}(g_{i+1}-g_{i})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{N-1}\sum_{i=\ell}^{N-1}2h_{i,\ell}\left\langle g_{\ell},g_{N}+u_{i}(g_{i+1}-g_{i})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{i}2h_{i,\ell}\left\langle g_{\ell},g_{N}+u_{i}(g_{i+1}-g_{i})\right\rangle.

(40)

Finally, changing the name of the indices
(i,\ell)
to
(k,j+1)
in (
40
) gives

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{i}2h_{i,\ell}\left\langle g_{\ell},g_{N}+u_{i}(g_{i+1}-g_{i})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}2h_{k,j+1}\left\langle g_{j+1},g_{N}+u_{k}(g_{k+1}-g_{k})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\left\langle g_{N}+u_{k}(g_{k+1}-g_{k}),\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}2h_{k,j+1}g_{j+1}\right\rangle

where the last expression coincides with the last summation within (
36
).
This shows that
{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{F}}(g_{1},\dots,g_{N}))={\mathcal{T}}(g_{1}^{\prime},\dots,g_{N}^{\prime})={\mathcal{S}}(g_{1},\dots,g_{N})
, completing the proof of
Theorem

D.1
.

Appendix E
Proof of the optimal family theorem

E.1
Overview of the proof and description of the parametrization
\Phi\colon C\to\mathbb{R}^{(N-1)\times(N-1)}

In this section, we prove
Theorem

4.1
.
The full proof is long and complicated, so we first outline the structure of the proof.

1.

We consider the following proof strategy: If there is some index set
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,N\}\times\{1,\dots,N\}
and nonnegative real numbers
\lambda_{i,j}
for each
(i,j)\in I
such that

\displaystyle 0=\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},x_{N}-y_{0}\right\rangle+N\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\|^{2}+\sum_{(i,j)\in I}\lambda_{i,j}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{i}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{j},x_{i}-x_{j}\right\rangle

(41)

then the algorithm exhibits the rate

\displaystyle\left\|y_{N-1}-{\mathbb{T}}y_{N-1}\right\|^{2}=4\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{4\left\|y_{0}-y_{\star}\right\|^{2}}{N^{2}}.

The final convergence rate is a direct consequence of (
41
), monotonicity of
{\mathbb{A}}
and
Lemma

3.2
.

2.

We choose
I=\{(k+1,k)\,|\,k=1,\dots,N-1\}\cup\{(N,k)\,|\,k=1,\dots,N-1\}
, and we use the H-matrix representation (
34
) to eliminate
x_{1},\dots,x_{N}
within (
41
).
Then (
41
) becomes a vector quadratic form in
\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1},\dots,\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},x_{N}-y_{0}\right\rangle+N\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\|^{2}+\sum_{(i,j)\in I}\lambda_{i,j}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{i}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{j},x_{i}-x_{j}\right\rangle=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=k}^{N}s_{\ell,k}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{\ell},\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}\right\rangle

where the coefficients
s_{\ell,k}=s_{\ell,k}\left(H,\lambda\right)
are functions depending on
H
and
\lambda=\left(\lambda_{i,j}\right)_{(i,j)\in I}

(note that we only keep
s_{\ell,k}
with
\ell\geq k
to avoid redundancy).
We characterize the explicit expressions for
s_{\ell,k}
in terms of
\lambda
and entries of
H
, reducing the problem of establishing the identity (
41
) to the problem of solving the system of equations

\displaystyle s_{\ell,k}\left(H,\lambda\right)=0\quad(k=1,\dots,N,\,\,\ell=k,\dots,N).

(42)

3.

We provide explicit solutions for
\lambda
, in terms of diagonal entries
h_{k,k}
(
k=1,\dots,N-1
) of the H-matrix.
With these explicit values of
\lambda
, the system (
42
) becomes nonlinear in
h_{k,k}
(
k=1,\dots,N-1
), but it is a linear system in non-diagonal entries
h_{\ell,k}
(
\ell=k+1,\dots,N-1
) of
H
.

4.

(This is the most technical core step of analysis.) We show that with the expressions of
\lambda
from the previous step, under certain conditions on
h_{k,k}
(
k=1,\dots,N-1
), the linear system (
42
) is uniquely solvable in the non-diagonal H-matrix entries
h_{\ell,k}
(
\ell=k+1,\dots,N-1
), and these unique solutions

\displaystyle h_{\ell,k}^{*}=h_{\ell,k}^{*}(h_{1,1},h_{2,2},\dots,h_{N-1,N-1})

(43)

can be expressed as continuous functions of the diagonal
h_{k,k}
(
k=1,\dots,N-1
).

We now describe how
\Phi\colon C\to\mathbb{R}^{(N-1)\times(N-1)}
is constructed.
Define

\displaystyle p_{k}=\prod_{\ell=k}^{N-1}h_{\ell,\ell},\quad k=1,\dots,N-1

(44)

(these quantities play significant role in all analyses of this section).
It turns out that the affine constraints

\displaystyle p_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}

(45)

\displaystyle p_{k}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{N-k+1}\quad(k=2,\dots,N-1)

(46)

\displaystyle p_{k}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{N-k}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}-\frac{1}{N-k-1}\quad(k=1,\dots,N-2)

(47)

are the key conditions making the construction of Steps 1 through 4 work.
Specifically, we have
\lambda_{i,j}>0
for explicit expressions of
\lambda
from Step 3, if the constraints (
45
) holds, and (
46
) and (
47
) holds with strict inequality.
The set
C
of all
(p_{2},\dots,p_{N-1})
satisfying the inequality constraints (
46
) and (
47
) strictly is an open convex subset of
\mathbb{R}^{N-2}
.
To check that
C\neq\emptyset
, consider the H-matrices of
OHM
and
Dual-OHM
.
From
OHM
, we have

\displaystyle p_{k}^{(0)}=\prod_{\ell=k}^{N-1}h_{\ell,\ell}=\prod_{\ell=k}^{N-1}\frac{\ell}{\ell+1}=\frac{k}{N}

and from
Dual-OHM
,

\displaystyle p_{k}^{(1)}=\prod_{\ell=k}^{N-1}h_{\ell,\ell}=\prod_{\ell=k}^{N-1}\frac{N-\ell}{N-\ell+1}=\frac{1}{N-k+1}.

It can be checked by direct calculations that
p_{1}^{(0)}=\frac{1}{N}=p_{1}^{(1)}
and that
p_{k}^{(0)}
’s satisfy (
46
) strictly and (
47
) with equality, while
p_{k}^{(1)}
’s satisfy (
46
) with equality and (
47
) strictly.
Therefore,
\left(p_{2}^{(0)},\dots,p_{N-1}^{(0)}\right),\left(p_{2}^{(0)},\dots,p_{N-1}^{(0)}\right)\in\partial C
.
Additionally, if we define
p_{k}^{(\gamma)}:=\gamma p_{k}^{(0)}+(1-\gamma)p_{k}^{(1)}
for
\gamma\in(0,1)
and
k=1,\dots,N-1
, then
p_{1}^{(\gamma)}=\frac{1}{N}
, and
\left(p_{2}^{(\gamma)},\dots,p_{N-1}^{(\gamma)}\right)
satisfies both (
46
), (
47
) strictly, i.e.,

\displaystyle\left(p_{2}^{(\gamma)},\dots,p_{N-1}^{(\gamma)}\right)\in C.

This shows that
C
is nonempty.

Note that provided that
p_{1}=\frac{1}{N}
, we can recover the diagonal
h_{k,k}
’s from
(p_{2},\dots,p_{N-1})\in C
as
h_{1,1}=\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}=\frac{1}{Np_{2}}
,
h_{k,k}=\frac{p_{k}}{p_{k+1}}
for
k=2,\dots,N-2
and
h_{N-1,N-1}=p_{N-1}
.
Now assuming that Step 4 is done so that we have
h_{\ell,k}^{*}
determined as functions of
h_{1,1},\dots,h_{N-1,N-1}
as in (
43
),
we define
\Phi:C\to\mathbb{R}^{(N-1)\times(N-1)}
by

\displaystyle\Phi(p_{2},\dots,p_{N-1})=\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{Np_{2}}&0&\cdots&0\\
h_{2,1}^{*}\left(\frac{1}{Np_{2}},\frac{p_{2}}{p_{3}},\dots,p_{N-1}\right)&\frac{p_{2}}{p_{3}}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
h_{N-1,1}^{*}\left(\frac{1}{Np_{2}},\frac{p_{2}}{p_{3}},\dots,p_{N-1}\right)&h_{N-1,2}^{*}\left(\frac{1}{Np_{2}},\frac{p_{2}}{p_{3}},\dots,p_{N-1}\right)&\cdots&p_{N-1}\end{bmatrix}.

This map is injective because one can recover the values of
p_{2},\dots,p_{N-1}
from the diagonal entries of
\Phi(p_{2},\dots,p_{N-1})
.
Additionally, it is continuous provided that
h_{\ell,k}^{*}
are continuous.
Because
\Phi(w)
is designed to satisfy (
41
) (when it is used as H-matrix) for any
w=(p_{2},\dots,p_{N-1})\in C
, by monotonicity of
{\mathbb{A}}
, the algorithm with
\Phi(w)
as H-matrix exhibits the exact optimal rate of
\left\|y_{N-1}-{\mathbb{T}}y_{N-1}\right\|^{2}=4\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{4\left\|y_{0}-y_{\star}\right\|^{2}}{N^{2}}
by Step 1.
In the subsequent sections, we go through each steps outlined above, and explain how the quantities
p_{k}
defined in (
44
) and the constraints (
45
), (
46
) and (
47
) become relevant to analysis.

E.2
Step 2: Computation of coefficient functions
s_{\ell,k}
in vector quadratic form

We rewrite the terms
x_{N}-y_{0}
and
x_{i}-x_{j}
in the right hand side of (
41
) as linear combinations of
\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}
(
k=1,\dots,N
) according to the definition (
6
) and
x_{k}={\mathbb{J}}_{{\mathbb{A}}}(y_{k-1})=y_{k-1}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}
, and then expand everything.
Denoting
g_{k}=\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}
for simplicity, the expansion goes:

\displaystyle\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},x_{N}-y_{0}\right\rangle+N\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\|^{2}+\sum_{(i,j)\in I}\lambda_{i,j}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{i}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{j},x_{i}-x_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},x_{N}-y_{0}\right\rangle+N\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\|^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{N-2}\lambda_{k+1,k}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+1}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k},x_{k+1}-x_{k}\right\rangle+\sum_{k=1}^{N-2}\lambda_{N,k}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k},x_{N}-x_{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda_{N,N-1}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N-1},x_{N}-x_{N-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle g_{N},-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\sum_{j=1}^{k}2h_{k,j}g_{j}-g_{N}\right\rangle+N\|g_{N}\|^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{N-2}\lambda_{k+1,k}\left\langle g_{k+1}-g_{k},-\sum_{j=1}^{k}2h_{k,j}g_{j}-(g_{k+1}-g_{k})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{N-2}\lambda_{N,k}\left\langle g_{N}-g_{k},-\sum_{i=k}^{N-1}\sum_{j=1}^{i}2h_{i,j}g_{j}-(g_{N}-g_{k})\right\rangle+\lambda_{N,N-1}\left\langle g_{N}-g_{N-1},-\sum_{j=1}^{N-1}2h_{N-1,j}g_{j}-(g_{N}-g_{N-1})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle g_{N},-\sum_{j=1}^{N-1}\sum_{k=j}^{N-1}2h_{k,j}g_{j}-g_{N}\right\rangle+N\|g_{N}\|^{2}-\sum_{k=1}^{N-2}\lambda_{k+1,k}\|g_{k+1}-g_{k}\|^{2}-\sum_{k=1}^{N-2}\lambda_{N,k}\|g_{N}-g_{k}\|^{2}-\lambda_{N,N-1}\|g_{N}-g_{N-1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{k=1}^{N-2}\lambda_{N,k}\left\langle g_{N}-g_{k},\sum_{i=k}^{N-1}\sum_{j=1}^{i}2h_{i,j}g_{j}\right\rangle-\sum_{j=1}^{N-2}\left\langle\sum_{k=j}^{N-2}2h_{k,j}\lambda_{k+1,k}(g_{k+1}-g_{k}),g_{j}\right\rangle-\lambda_{N,N-1}\left\langle g_{N}-g_{N-1},\sum_{j=1}^{N-1}2h_{N-1,j}g_{j}\right\rangle.

We carefully gather the terms within the above expansion and group the coefficients attached to the same inner product terms, into the form

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=k}^{N}s_{\ell,k}\left\langle g_{\ell},g_{k}\right\rangle.

(48)

The result of the computation is as following:

\displaystyle\begin{split}s_{N,N}&=N-1-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\lambda_{N,k}\\
s_{N-1,N-1}&=-\lambda_{N-1,N-2}+\lambda_{N,N-1}(2h_{N-1,N-1}-1)\\
s_{N,N-1}&=-2h_{N-1,N-1}-\sum_{k=1}^{N-2}2\lambda_{N,k}h_{N-1,N-1}-2\lambda_{N,N-1}(h_{N-1,N-1}-1)\end{split}

(49)

and for
k=1,\dots,N-2
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}s_{k,k}&=\begin{cases}\lambda_{k+1,k}(2h_{k,k}-1)-\lambda_{k,k-1}+\lambda_{N,k}\left(\sum_{i=k}^{N-1}2h_{i,k}-1\right)&\text{if }\,\,k>1\\
\lambda_{k+1,k}(2h_{k,k}-1)+\lambda_{N,k}\left(\sum_{i=k}^{N-1}2h_{i,k}-1\right)&\text{if }\,\,k=1\end{cases}\\
s_{k+1,k}&=2\lambda_{k+1,k}(1-h_{k,k})+2\lambda_{k+2,k+1}h_{k+1,k}+2\lambda_{N,k}\sum_{i=k+1}^{N-1}h_{i,k+1}+2\lambda_{N,k+1}\sum_{i=k+1}^{N-1}h_{i,k}\quad(k<N-2)\\
s_{\ell,k}&=-2\lambda_{\ell,\ell-1}h_{\ell-1,k}+2\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}h_{\ell,k}+2\lambda_{N,k}\sum_{i=\ell}^{N-1}h_{i,\ell}+2\lambda_{N,\ell}\sum_{i=\ell}^{N-1}h_{i,k}\quad(\ell=k+2,\dots,N-2)\\
s_{N-1,k}&=2\lambda_{N,k}h_{N-1,N-1}-2\lambda_{N-1,N-2}h_{N-2,k}+2\lambda_{N,N-1}h_{N-1,k}\quad(k<N-2)\\
s_{N,k}&=2\lambda_{N,k}-\sum_{\ell=k}^{N-1}\left(1+\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\lambda_{N,j}\right)2h_{\ell,k}\end{split}

(50)

and finally,

\displaystyle s_{N-1,N-2}=2\lambda_{N-1,N-2}(1-h_{N-2,N-2})+2\lambda_{N,N-1}h_{N-1,N-2}+2\lambda_{N,N-2}h_{N-1,N-1}.

(51)

E.3
Step 3: Explicit characterization of
\lambda

Recall the definition (
44
):
p_{k}=\prod_{\ell=k}^{N-1}h_{\ell,\ell}
for
k=1,\dots,N-1
.
Assume
p_{1}=\frac{1}{N}
.
We define

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lambda_{N,N-1}&=Nh_{N-1,N-1}\\
\lambda_{k+1,k}&=\frac{N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}\left((N-k)p_{k+1}-1\right)\quad(k=1,\dots,N-2)\\
\lambda_{N,k}&=\frac{N}{(N-k)(N-k-1)}-\frac{N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}+\frac{N}{N-k}p_{k}\quad(k=1,\dots,N-2)\end{split}

(52)

We prove the following handy identities for
\lambda
.

Proposition E.1
.

For
\lambda
defined as in (
52
), the following holds, provided that
p_{1}=\frac{1}{N}
.

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k}^{N-1}\lambda_{N,i}=\frac{N(N-k-1)}{N-k}+\frac{N}{N-k}p_{k}\quad(k=2,\dots,N-1)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\lambda_{N,i}=N-1.

Proof.

Observe that the first identity, when
k=N-1
, is
\lambda_{N,N-1}=Np_{N-1}=Nh_{N-1,N-1}
, which holds true by definition.
Next, for
k=1,\dots,N-1
, we can rewrite

\displaystyle\lambda_{N,k}=\frac{N}{N-k-1}-\frac{N}{N-k}-\frac{N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}+\frac{N}{N-k}p_{k}

so they telescope:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k}^{N-1}\lambda_{N,i}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{N,N-1}+\sum_{i=k}^{N-2}\lambda_{N,i}

\displaystyle=Np_{N-1}+\sum_{i=k}^{N-2}\left(\frac{N}{N-i-1}-\frac{N}{N-i}-\frac{N}{N-i-1}p_{i+1}+\frac{N}{N-i}p_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=Np_{N-1}+\left(N-\frac{N}{N-k}-Np_{N-1}+\frac{N}{N-k}p_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{N(N-k-1)}{N-k}+\frac{N}{N-k}p_{k}.

In the case
k=1
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\lambda_{N,i}=\frac{N(N-2)}{N-1}+\frac{N}{N-1}p_{1}=\frac{N^{2}-2N+1}{N-1}=N-1.

∎

Observe that
\lambda_{k+1,k}\geq 0
if
p_{k+1}\geq\frac{1}{N-k}
for
k=1,\dots,N-2
, which is equivalent to (
46
).
Moreover,

\displaystyle\lambda_{N,k}=\frac{N}{(N-k)(N-k-1)}-\frac{N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}+\frac{N}{N-k}p_{k}\geq 0

\displaystyle\iff p_{k}\geq\frac{N-k}{N}\left(\frac{N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}-\frac{N}{(N-k)(N-k-1)}\right)=\frac{N-k}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}-\frac{1}{N-k-1}

which is (
47
).
Clearly, if these inequalities are satisfied strictly, then
\lambda_{k+1,k},\lambda_{N,k}>0
.

E.4
Step 4: Solving the linear system
s_{\ell,k}=0

We first observe that by definition of
\lambda
from the previous section and
Proposition

E.1
,

\displaystyle s_{N,N}

\displaystyle=N-1-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\lambda_{N,k}=0

\displaystyle s_{N-1,N-1}

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{N-1,N-2}+\lambda_{N,N-1}(2h_{N-1,N-1}-1)

\displaystyle=-Np_{N-1}(2p_{N-1}-1)+Nh_{N-1,N-1}(2h_{N-1,N-1}-1)=0

and

\displaystyle s_{N,N-1}

\displaystyle=-2h_{N-1,N-1}-\sum_{k=1}^{N-2}2\lambda_{N,k}h_{N-1,N-1}-2\lambda_{N,N-1}(h_{N-1,N-1}-1)

\displaystyle=-2h_{N-1,N-1}\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\lambda_{N,k}\right)+2\lambda_{N,N-1}

\displaystyle=-2Nh_{N-1,N-1}+2\lambda_{N,N-1}

\displaystyle=0

directly follows.
However, for
s_{\ell,k}
with
k\leq N-2
and
\ell=k,\dots,N
, we need to determine
h_{k+1,k},\dots,h_{N-1,k}
that achieves
s_{\ell,k}=0
.
We first characterize the condition for
s_{k,k}=0
.

Proposition E.2
.

For
k=1,\dots,N-2
, provided that
p_{1}=\frac{1}{N}
,

\displaystyle s_{k,k}=0\iff\sum_{i=k}^{N-1}2h_{i,k}=1-(N-k)p_{k+1}+(N-k+1)p_{k}.

(53)

Proof.

Recall from (
50
) that

\displaystyle s_{k,k}=\begin{cases}\lambda_{k+1,k}(2h_{k,k}-1)-\lambda_{k,k-1}+\lambda_{N,k}\left(\sum_{i=k}^{N-1}2h_{i,k}-1\right)&\text{if }\,\,k>1\\
\lambda_{k+1,k}(2h_{k,k}-1)+\lambda_{N,k}\left(\sum_{i=k}^{N-1}2h_{i,k}-1\right)&\text{if }\,\,k=1\end{cases}

To eliminate the need to deal with the case
k=1
separately, define the dummy variable
\lambda_{1,0}=\frac{N}{N-1}p_{1}(Np_{1}-1)
, which is consistent with the rule (
52
) for defining
\lambda_{k+1,k}
for
k=1,\dots,N-2
.
Given that
p_{1}=\frac{1}{N}
we have
\lambda_{1,0}=0
, so we can write

\displaystyle s_{k,k}=0\,\iff\,\sum_{i=k}^{N-1}2h_{i,k}-1=\frac{\lambda_{k,k-1}-\lambda_{k+1,k}(2h_{k,k}-1)}{\lambda_{N,k}}

(54)

where

\displaystyle\lambda_{k+1,k}

\displaystyle=\frac{N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}\left((N-k)p_{k+1}-1\right)

\displaystyle\lambda_{k,k-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{N}{N-k}p_{k}\left((N-k+1)p_{k}-1\right)

\displaystyle\lambda_{N,k}

\displaystyle=\frac{N}{(N-k)(N-k-1)}-\frac{N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}+\frac{N}{N-k}p_{k}

\displaystyle h_{k,k}

\displaystyle=\frac{\prod_{\ell=k}^{N-1}h_{\ell,\ell}}{\prod_{\ell=k+1}^{N-1}h_{\ell,\ell}}=\frac{p_{k}}{p_{k+1}}

Plugging the above identities into the right hand side of (
54
) gives

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k}^{N-1}2h_{i,k}-1

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda_{k,k-1}-\lambda_{k+1,k}(2h_{k,k}-1)}{\lambda_{N,k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{N}{N-k}p_{k}\left((N-k+1)p_{k}-1\right)-\frac{N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}\left((N-k)p_{k+1}-1\right)\left(\frac{2p_{k}}{p_{k+1}}-1\right)}{\frac{N}{(N-k)(N-k-1)}-\frac{N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}+\frac{N}{N-k}p_{k}}.

Multiply
\frac{(N-k)(N-k-1)}{N}
throughout both numerator and denominator, and rearrange the expressions using the substitution
d_{k}=1-(N-k)p_{k+1}+(N-k-1)p_{k}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k}^{N-1}2h_{i,k}-1

\displaystyle=\frac{(N-k-1)p_{k}\left((N-k+1)p_{k}-1\right)-(N-k)\left((N-k)p_{k+1}-1\right)\left(2p_{k}-p_{k+1}\right)}{1-(N-k)p_{k+1}+(N-k-1)p_{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(N-k-1)p_{k}\left((N-k+1)p_{k}-1\right)-\left((N-k)p_{k+1}-1\right)\left(2(N-k)p_{k}-(N-k)p_{k+1}\right)}{1-(N-k)p_{k+1}+(N-k-1)p_{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(N-k-1)p_{k}\left((N-k+1)p_{k}-1\right)-\left((N-k-1)p_{k}-d_{k}\right)\left(2(N-k)p_{k}+d_{k}-1-(N-k-1)p_{k}\right)}{d_{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(N-k-1)p_{k}\left((N-k+1)p_{k}-1\right)-\left((N-k-1)p_{k}-d_{k}\right)\left((N-k+1)p_{k}+d_{k}-1\right)}{d_{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{d_{k}^{2}+(2p_{k}-1)d_{k}}{d_{k}}

\displaystyle=d_{k}+2p_{k}-1.

This is again equivalent to

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k}^{N-1}2h_{i,k}=d_{k}+2p_{k}=1-(N-k)p_{k+1}+(N-k+1)p_{k}.

∎

We name the quantity

\displaystyle q_{k}=1-(N-k)p_{k+1}+(N-k+1)p_{k}.

(55)

from (
53
) as it will be frequently used in the subsequent analysis.

In (
50
), we see that for each fixed
k=1,\dots,N-2
, the equations
s_{\ell,k}=0
(
\ell\geq k
) involve only
h_{i,j}
’s with
i\geq j\geq k
.
Therefore, rather than trying to solve the whole system
s_{\ell,j}=0
(
j=1,\dots,N-2
,
\ell=k,\dots,N
) at once, we iteratively solve the partial systems
s_{\ell,k}=0
(
\ell=k,\dots,N
) one-by-one with fixed value of
k
, starting from
k=N-2
and progressively lowering it to
k=1
.

Proposition E.3
.

For each
k=1,\dots,N-2
fixed, the system of equations
s_{\ell,k}=0
(
\ell=k,\dots,N
), viewing only
h_{k+1,k},\dots,h_{N-1,k}
as variables, is a uniquely solvable linear system, if: all
\lambda_{i,j}
’s defined in (
52
) are positive and

\displaystyle\sum_{i=j}^{N-1}2h_{i,j}=q_{j}

(56)

holds for
j=k+1,\dots,N-2
.
In this case, the solutions
h_{k+1,k}^{*},\dots,h_{N-1,k}^{*}
satisfying
s_{\ell,k}=0
(
\ell=k,\dots,N
) can be written as continuous functions of
h_{1,1},\dots,h_{N-1,N-1}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle h_{i,k}^{*}=h_{i,k}^{*}\left(h_{1,1},\dots,h_{N-1,N-1}\right).

Proof.

First consider the case
k=N-2
, which requires a separate treatment because
s_{N-1,N-2}
is defined separately as (
51
).
In this case, we have 3 equations to solve, namely

\displaystyle s_{N-2,N-2}=0,\quad s_{N-1,N-2}=0,\quad s_{N,N-2}=0.

(57)

By
Proposition

E.2
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}s_{N-2,N-2}=0\,&\iff\,2(h_{N-2,N-2}+h_{N-1,N-2})=q_{N-1}=1-2p_{N-1}+3p_{N-2}\\
&\iff\,h_{N-1,N-2}=\frac{1-2h_{N-1}+3h_{N-2,N-2}h_{N-1,N-1}}{2}-h_{N-2,N-2}.\end{split}

(58)

Plug the last expression for
h_{N-1,N-2}
into

\displaystyle s_{N-1,N-2}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{N-1,N-2}(1-h_{N-2,N-2})+2\lambda_{N,N-1}h_{N-1,N-2}+2\lambda_{N,N-2}h_{N-1,N-1}

\displaystyle=2Nh_{N-1,N-1}(2h_{N-1,N-1}-1)(1-h_{N-2,N-2})+2Nh_{N-1,N-1}h_{N-1,N-2}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\left(\frac{N}{2}-Nh_{N-1,N-1}+\frac{N}{2}h_{N-2,N-2}h_{9}{N-1,N-1}\right)h_{N-1,N-1}

gives
s_{N-1,N-2}=0
.
Next, provided that (
58
) holds true, we have

\displaystyle s_{N,N-2}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{N,N-2}-\left(1+\sum_{i=1}^{N-2}\lambda_{N,i}\right)2h_{N-2,k}-\left(1+\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\lambda_{N,i}\right)2h_{N-1,k}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{N,N-2}-(N-\lambda_{N,N-1})2h_{N-2,N-2}-2Nh_{N-1,N-2}

\displaystyle=2\left(\frac{N}{2}-Nh_{N-1,N-1}+\frac{N}{2}h_{N-2,N-2}h_{N-1,N-1}\right)+2\lambda_{N,N-1}h_{N-2,N-2}-N(2h_{N-2,N-2}+2h_{N-1,N-1})

\displaystyle=2\left(\frac{N}{2}-Nh_{N-1,N-1}+\frac{N}{2}h_{N-2,N-2}h_{N-1,N-1}\right)+2Nh_{N-1,N-1}h_{N-2,N-2}-N(1-2h_{N-1}+3h_{N-2,N-2}h_{N-1,N-1})

\displaystyle=0.

This shows the value of
h_{N-1,N-2}
characterized by (
58
) is the unique solution solving the equations (
57
).
The expression determining
h_{N-1,N-2}
is clearly continuous in
h_{N-2,N-2},h_{N-1,N-1}
.

Now suppose
k<N-2
.
We first consider the following system of equations (without
s_{N-1,k}=0
and
s_{N,k}=0
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}s_{k,k}&=\lambda_{N,k}\left(\sum_{i=k+1}^{N-1}2h_{i,k}+2h_{k,k}-q_{k}\right)=0\\
s_{k+1,k}&=2\lambda_{k+1,k}(1-h_{k,k})+2\lambda_{k+2,k+1}h_{k+1,k}+2\lambda_{N,k}\sum_{i=k+1}^{N-1}h_{i,k+1}+2\lambda_{N,k+1}\sum_{i=k+1}^{N-1}h_{i,k}=0\\
s_{\ell,k}&=-2\lambda_{\ell,\ell-1}h_{\ell-1,k}+2\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}h_{\ell,k}+2\lambda_{N,k}\sum_{i=\ell}^{N-1}h_{i,\ell}+2\lambda_{N,\ell}\sum_{i=\ell}^{N-1}h_{i,k}=0\quad(\ell=k+2,\dots,N-2)\end{split}

(59)

where the first identity has been reorganized in a simpler form using
Proposition

E.2
.
We show that the system (
59
) form an invertible linear system in the variables
h_{k+1,k},\dots,h_{N-1,k}
, and thus have unique solutions.
Then we show that these solutions are consistent with the remaining two equations to complete the proof.

Claim 1.
The system of equations (
59
) is uniquely solvable.

In (
59
), the non-diagonal
h_{i,j}
’s with
j=k+1,\dots,N-2
are involved only through the summation
\sum_{i=j}^{N-1}h_{i,j}
, which can be replaced with
q_{j}/2
by the assumption (
56
).
Now for each
\ell=k+1,\dots,N-2
we subtract a multiple of
s_{k,k}
from each
s_{\ell,k}
to eliminate the variables
h_{\ell,k},\dots,h_{N-1,k}
while retaining an equivalent system of linear equations:

\displaystyle s^{\prime}_{k+1,k}

\displaystyle:=s_{k+1,k}-\frac{\lambda_{N,k+1}}{\lambda_{N,k}}s_{k,k}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{k+2,k+1}h_{k+1,k}+\underbrace{\lambda_{N,k}q_{k+1}+2\lambda_{k+1,k}(1-h_{k,k})+\lambda_{N,k+1}(q_{k}-2h_{k,k})}_{:=t_{k+1,k}=\,\text{Terms not depending on }h_{k+1,k},\dots,h_{N-1,k}}

\displaystyle s^{\prime}_{\ell,k}

\displaystyle:=s_{\ell,k}-\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}}{\lambda_{N,k}}s_{k,k}

\displaystyle=-2\lambda_{\ell,\ell-1}h_{\ell-1,k}+2\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}h_{\ell,k}-2\lambda_{N,\ell}\sum_{i=k+1}^{\ell-1}h_{i,k}+\underbrace{\lambda_{N,\ell}(q_{k}-2h_{k,k})+\lambda_{N,k}q_{\ell}}_{:=t_{\ell,k}=\,\text{Terms not depending on }h_{k+1,k},\dots,h_{N-1,k}}\quad(\ell=k+2,\dots,N-2).

For the sake of conciseness, we separately define the terms within
s^{\prime}_{\ell,k}
not depending on
h_{k+1,k},\dots,h_{N-1,k}
:

\displaystyle t_{\ell,k}=\begin{cases}\lambda_{N,k}q_{k+1}+2\lambda_{k+1,k}(1-h_{k,k})+\lambda_{N,k+1}(q_{k}-2h_{k,k})&\text{if }\ell=k+1\\
\lambda_{N,\ell}(q_{k}-2h_{k,k})+\lambda_{N,k}q_{\ell}&\text{if }\ell=k+2,\dots,N-2\end{cases}

(60)

so that we can write

\displaystyle s^{\prime}_{k+1,k}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{k+2,k+1}h_{k+1,k}+t_{k+1,k}

\displaystyle s^{\prime}_{\ell,k}

\displaystyle=-2\lambda_{\ell,\ell-1}h_{\ell-1,k}+2\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}h_{\ell,k}-2\lambda_{N,\ell}\sum_{i=k+1}^{\ell-1}h_{i,k}+t_{\ell,k}.

Next, we express the system of equations
\frac{s^{\prime}_{\ell,k}}{2}=0
(
\ell=k+1,\dots,N-1
) in matrix form.
To do this, we define the vectors within
\mathbb{R}^{N-k-1}
, holding the coefficients attached to the variables
h_{k+1,k},\dots,h_{N-1,k}
within each
\frac{s^{\prime}_{\ell,k}}{2}
:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}{\mathbf{a}}_{1}&=\big[&\lambda_{k+2,k+1}&0&0&\cdots&0&0&0\big]^{\intercal}\\
{\mathbf{a}}_{2}&=\big[&-\lambda_{N,k+2}-\lambda_{k+2,k+1}&\lambda_{k+3,k+2}&0&\cdots&0&0&0\big]^{\intercal}\\
{\mathbf{a}}_{3}&=\big[&-\lambda_{N,k+3}&-\lambda_{N,k+3}-\lambda_{k+3,k+2}&\lambda_{k+4,k+3}&\cdots&0&0&0\big]^{\intercal}\\
\vdots&&&&\vdots&&&\\
{\mathbf{a}}_{N-k-2}&=\big[&-\lambda_{N,N-2}&-\lambda_{N,N-2}&-\lambda_{N,N-2}&\cdots&-\lambda_{N,N-2}-\lambda_{N-2,N-3}&\lambda_{N-1,N-2}&0\big]^{\intercal}\end{array}

so that if we define
{\mathbf{h}}=\begin{bmatrix}h_{k+1,k}&\cdots&h_{N-1,k}\end{bmatrix}^{\intercal}
, then

\displaystyle\frac{s^{\prime}_{\ell,k}}{2}={\mathbf{a}}_{\ell-k}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{h}}+\frac{t_{\ell,k}}{2}

(61)

for
\ell=k+1,\dots,N-2
.
More precisely,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathbf{a}}_{1}&=\lambda_{k+2,k+1}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\\
{\mathbf{a}}_{\ell-k}&=-\lambda_{N,\ell}\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-k-1}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}-\lambda_{\ell,\ell-1}{\mathbf{e}}_{\ell-k-1}+\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}{\mathbf{e}}_{\ell-k},\quad\ell=k+2,\dots,N-2\end{split}

(62)

where
{\mathbf{e}}_{1},\dots,{\mathbf{e}}_{N-k-2}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-k-1}
are elementary basis vectors.
Finally, define

{\mathbf{a}}_{N-k-1}=\sum_{i=1}^{N-k-1}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}=\begin{bmatrix}1&1&\cdots&1\end{bmatrix}

so that

\displaystyle\frac{s_{k,k}}{2\lambda_{N,k}}=\sum_{i=k+1}^{N-1}h_{i,k}+h_{k,k}-\frac{q_{k}}{2}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{a}}_{N-k-1}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{h}}+h_{k,k}-\frac{q_{k}}{2}.

(63)

Now, by defining the matrix

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{a}}_{1}&{\mathbf{a}}_{2}&\cdots&{\mathbf{a}}_{N-k-1}\end{bmatrix}^{\intercal}\in\mathbb{R}^{(N-k-1)\times(N-k-1)}

we can write

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}s_{k,k}\\
s_{k+1,k}\\
\vdots\\
s_{N-2,k}\end{bmatrix}=0\,\iff\,\frac{1}{2}\begin{bmatrix}s_{k+1,k}^{\prime}\\
\vdots\\
s_{N-2,k}^{\prime}\\
s_{k,k}/\lambda_{N,k}\end{bmatrix}=0\,\iff\,{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{h}}=\frac{1}{2}\begin{bmatrix}-t_{k+1,k}\\
\vdots\\
-t_{N-2,k}\\
q_{k}-2h_{k,k}\end{bmatrix}.

Note that
{\mathbf{A}}
is lower-triangular:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}=\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{k+2,k+1}&0&\cdots&0&0\\
-\lambda_{N,k+2}-\lambda_{k+2,k+1}&\lambda_{k+3,k+2}&\cdots&0&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
-\lambda_{N,N-2}&-\lambda_{N,N-2}&\cdots&\lambda_{N-1,N-2}&0\\
1&1&\cdots&1&1\end{bmatrix}

Therefore, it is invertible provided that all diagonal entries
\lambda_{k+2,k+1},\dots,\lambda_{N-1,N-2}
are positive.
Because all entries of
{\mathbf{A}}
and
t_{k+1,k},\dots,t_{N-2,k}
are expressed as continuous functions of
h_{1,1},\dots,h_{N-1,N-1}
(because
\lambda
is defined by (
52
) and
q_{\ell}
’s are defined by (
55
)), applying Cramer’s rule to the above system, we can write
h_{k+1,k},\dots,h_{N-1,k}
as continuous functions of
h_{k,k},\dots,h_{N-1,N-1}
.

Claim 2.
Assuming that the solutions of (
59
) also satisfy
s_{N-1,k}=0
, we have
s_{N,k}=0
.

Recall that

\displaystyle s_{N-1,k}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{N,k}h_{N-1,N-1}-2\lambda_{N-1,N-2}h_{N-2,k}+2\lambda_{N,N-1}h_{N-1,k}

\displaystyle s_{N,k}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{N,k}-\sum_{\ell=k}^{N-1}\left(1+\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\lambda_{N,j}\right)2h_{\ell,k}.

(64)

Assume
s_{N-1,k}=0
(as well as
s_{\ell,k}=0
for
\ell=k,\dots,N-2
).
Then

\displaystyle 0=s^{\prime}_{N-1,k}

\displaystyle:=s_{N-1,k}-\frac{\lambda_{N,N-1}}{\lambda_{N,k}}s_{k,k}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{N,k}h_{N-1,N-1}+2\lambda_{N,N-1}h_{N-1,k}-2\lambda_{N-1,N-2}h_{N-2,k}-\lambda_{N,N-1}\left(\sum_{i=k+1}^{N-1}2h_{i,k}+2h_{k,k}-q_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{N,N-1}\sum_{i=k+1}^{N-3}2h_{i,k}-(\lambda_{N,N-1}+\lambda_{N-1,N-2})2h_{N-2,k}+\underbrace{2\lambda_{N,k}h_{N-1,N-1}+\lambda_{N,N-1}(q_{k}-2h_{k,k})}_{:=t_{N-1,k}=\,\text{Terms not depending on }h_{k+1,k},\dots,h_{N-1,k}}.

Now sum up all
s^{\prime}_{\ell,k}
’s for
\ell=k+1,\dots,N-1
(up to scalar multiplication by 2, the coefficients of
h_{k+1,k},\dots,h_{N-1,k}
correspond to
{\mathbf{a}}_{1}+\cdots+{\mathbf{a}}_{N-k-2}
(recall (
62
)) plus the vector
\begin{bmatrix}-\lambda_{N,N-1}&\cdots&-\lambda_{N,N-1}&-\lambda_{N,N-1}-\lambda_{N-1,N-2}&0\end{bmatrix}
) to obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-1}s^{\prime}_{\ell,k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-1}\left(-\sum_{i=\ell+1}^{N-1}\lambda_{N,i}\right)2h_{\ell,k}+\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-1}t_{\ell,k}.

Using the fact
1+\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\lambda_{N,i}=N
(from Proposition
E.1
), if we add

\displaystyle 0=\frac{N}{\lambda_{N,k}}s_{k,k}

\displaystyle=\left(1+\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\lambda_{N,i}\right)\left(\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-1}2h_{\ell,k}+2h_{k,k}-q_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-1}\left(1+\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\lambda_{N,i}\right)2h_{\ell,k}+N(2h_{k,k}-q_{k})

to both sides, we get

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-1}\left(1+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\lambda_{N,i}\right)2h_{\ell,k}+N(2h_{k,k}-q_{k})+\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-1}t_{\ell,k}

\displaystyle\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eqn:appendix-optimal-family-sNk-definition}}}{{=}}-s_{N,k}+2\lambda_{N,k}-\left(1+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\lambda_{N,i}\right)2h_{k,k}+N(2h_{k,k}-q_{k})+\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-1}t_{\ell,k}

\displaystyle=-s_{N,k}+\underbrace{2\lambda_{N,k}+\left(\sum_{i=k+1}^{N-1}\lambda_{N,i}\right)2h_{k,k}-Nq_{k}+\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-1}t_{\ell,k}}_{:=t_{N,k}=\,\text{Terms not depending on }h_{k+1,k},\dots,h_{N-1,k}}.

(65)

We show that
t_{N,k}=0
, which then implies
s_{N,k}=0
.
We expand and rearrange the summation
\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-1}t_{\ell,k}
as following:

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-1}t_{\ell,k}

\displaystyle=t_{k+1,k}+\sum_{\ell=k+2}^{N-2}t_{\ell,k}+t_{N-1,k}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{N,k}q_{k+1}+2\lambda_{k+1,k}(1-h_{k,k})+\lambda_{N,k+1}(q_{k}-2h_{k,k})+\sum_{\ell=k+2}^{N-2}\left(\lambda_{N,\ell}(q_{k}-2h_{k,k})+\lambda_{N,k}q_{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+2\lambda_{N,k}h_{N-1,N-1}+\lambda_{N,N-1}(q_{k}-2h_{k,k})

\displaystyle=\lambda_{N,k}\left(\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-2}q_{\ell}+2h_{N-1,N-1}\right)+2\lambda_{k+1,k}(1-h_{k,k})+\left(\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-1}\lambda_{N,\ell}\right)(q_{k}-2h_{k,k}).

(66)

Because of the definition (
55
) of
q_{\ell}
, their sum telescopes:

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-2}q_{\ell}+2h_{N-1,N-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-2}1-(N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}+(N-\ell+1)p_{\ell}\right)+2h_{N-1,N-1}

\displaystyle=(N-k-2)-2p_{N-1}+(N-k)p_{k+1}+2h_{N-1,N-1}

\displaystyle=(N-k-2)+(N-k)p_{k+1}

(67)

where the last line follows from
p_{N-1}=\prod_{\ell=N-1}^{N-1}h_{\ell,\ell}=h_{N-1,N-1}
.
Now plugging in the expression (
66
) into (
65
) and applying simplification by (
67
) gives

\displaystyle t_{N,k}=\underbrace{\lambda_{N,k}\left((N-k)+(N-k)p_{k+1}\right)}_{(\mathrm{I})}+\underbrace{2\lambda_{k+1,k}(1-h_{k,k})}_{(\mathrm{II})}+\underbrace{\left(\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-1}\lambda_{N,\ell}-N\right)q_{k}}_{(\mathrm{III})}.

We expand each term
(\mathrm{I}),(\mathrm{II}),(\mathrm{III})
: first, plugging in the expression for
\lambda_{N,k}
from (
52
), we obtain

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I})

\displaystyle=(N-k)\lambda_{N,k}\left(1+p_{k+1}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{N}{N-k-1}-\frac{N(N-k)}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}+Np_{k}\right)(1+p_{k+1})

\displaystyle=\frac{N}{N-k-1}-\frac{N(N-k)}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}+Np_{k}+\frac{N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}-\frac{N(N-k)}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}^{2}+Np_{k}p_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{N}{N-k-1}+Np_{k}-Np_{k+1}+Np_{k}p_{k+1}-\frac{N(N-k)}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}^{2}.

(68)

Next, again from (
52
), plug in the expression for
\lambda_{k+1,k}
into
(\mathrm{II})
to get

\displaystyle(\mathrm{II})

\displaystyle=\frac{2N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}\left((N-k)p_{k+1}-1\right)(1-h_{k,k})

\displaystyle=\frac{2N(N-k)}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}^{2}-\frac{2N(N-k)}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}^{2}h_{k,k}-\frac{2N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}+\frac{2N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}h_{k,k}

\displaystyle=\frac{2N}{N-k-1}p_{k}-\frac{2N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}-\frac{2N(N-k)}{N-k-1}p_{k}p_{k+1}+\frac{2N(N-k)}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}^{2}

(69)

where in the last line, we use
p_{k}=\prod_{\ell=k}^{N-1}h_{\ell,\ell}=h_{k,k}\prod_{\ell=k+1}^{N-1}h_{\ell,\ell}=p_{k+1}h_{k,k}
.
Finally, applying
Proposition

E.1
, we rewrite
(\mathrm{III})
as

\displaystyle(\mathrm{III})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{N(N-k-2)}{N-k-1}+\frac{N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}-N\right)q_{k}

\displaystyle=\left(-\frac{N}{N-k-1}+\frac{N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}\right)\left(1-(N-k)p_{k+1}+(N-k+1)p_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{N}{N-k-1}+\frac{N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}+\frac{N(N-k)}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}-\frac{N(N-k)}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}^{2}-\frac{N(N-k+1)}{N-k-1}p_{k}+\frac{N(N-k+1)}{N-k-1}p_{k}p_{k+1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{N}{N-k-1}-\frac{N(N-k+1)}{N-k-1}p_{k}+\frac{N(N-k+1)}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}+\frac{N(N-k+1)}{N-k-1}p_{k}p_{k+1}-\frac{N(N-k)}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}^{2}.

(70)

Summing up the expressions (
68
), (
69
), (
70
), the coefficients of
p_{k},p_{k+1},p_{k}p_{k+1},p_{k+1}^{2}
terms and the constant term all vanish, and we conclude

\displaystyle t_{N,k}=(\mathrm{I})+(\mathrm{II})+(\mathrm{III})=0.

Claim 3.
The solutions of (
59
) satisfy
s_{N-1,k}=0
.

To prove this, we first have to match the coefficients of
h_{k+1,k},\dots,h_{N-1,k}
within
s_{N-1,k}
via linear combination of rows of
{\mathbf{A}}
through Gaussian elimination.
The following proposition and its proof outlines this process.

Proposition E.4
.

The following identity holds:

\displaystyle s_{N-1,k}-2\lambda_{N,k}h_{N-1,N-1}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{N,N-1}\left(\frac{1}{\lambda_{N,k}}s_{k,k}-2h_{k,k}+q_{k}\right)-\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-2}\frac{a_{\ell+1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}\left(s^{\prime}_{\ell,k}-t_{\ell,k}\right)

(71)

where the sequences
\{a_{\ell},b_{\ell}\}_{\ell=k+1}^{N-1}
are defined by
a_{N-1}=\lambda_{N,N-1}+\lambda_{N-1,N-2},b_{N-1}=\lambda_{N,N-1}
and the reverse recursion

\displaystyle\begin{split}a_{\ell}&=b_{\ell+1}+\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}+\lambda_{\ell,\ell-1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}a_{\ell+1}\\
b_{\ell}&=b_{\ell+1}+\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}a_{\ell+1}\end{split}

(72)

for
\ell=N-1,\dots,k+1
.

Proof.

Define
{\mathbf{v}}=\begin{bmatrix}0&\cdots&0&-\lambda_{N-1,N-2}&\lambda_{N,N-1}\end{bmatrix}^{\intercal}
, so that

\displaystyle s_{N-1,k}-2\lambda_{N,k}h_{N-1,N-1}

\displaystyle=-2\lambda_{N-1,N-2}h_{N-2,k}+2\lambda_{N,N-1}h_{N-1,k}=2{\mathbf{v}}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{h}}

Recall the vector identities (
61
), (
63
)

\displaystyle s^{\prime}_{\ell,k}-t_{\ell,k}

\displaystyle=2{\mathbf{a}}_{\ell-k}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{h}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda_{N,k}}s_{k,k}-2h_{k,k}+q_{k}

\displaystyle=2{\mathbf{a}}_{N-k-1}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{h}}

where
{\mathbf{a}}_{1}^{\intercal},\dots,{\mathbf{a}}_{N-k-1}^{\intercal}
are row vectors of
{\mathbf{A}}
.
Because
{\mathbf{A}}
is lower-triangular, we can express
{\mathbf{v}}
as a linear combination of
{\mathbf{a}}_{j}
by matching the entries from backwards.
We start with

\displaystyle{\mathbf{v}}_{N-1}:={\mathbf{v}}-\lambda_{N,N-1}{\mathbf{a}}_{N-k-1}=\begin{bmatrix}-\lambda_{N,N-1}&-\lambda_{N,N-1}&\cdots&-\lambda_{N,N-1}&-\lambda_{N,N-1}-\lambda_{N-1,N-2}&0\end{bmatrix}^{\intercal}.

We recursively define a sequence of vectors
{\mathbf{v}}_{\ell}
(
\ell=N-2,\dots,k+1
) by adding a constant multiple of
{\mathbf{a}}_{\ell-k}
from
{\mathbf{v}}_{\ell+1}
which eliminates the
(\ell-k)
th entry of
{\mathbf{v}}_{\ell+1}
.
We denote the value of the
(\ell-k)
th entry of
{\mathbf{v}}_{\ell+1}
by
-a_{\ell+1}
, and the common value of the remaining entries by
-b_{\ell+1}
, so:

\displaystyle a_{N-1}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{N,N-1}+\lambda_{N-1,N-2}

\displaystyle b_{N-1}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{N,N-1}.

Note that for
\ell=N-2,\dots,k+1
, the last nonzero entry of
{\mathbf{a}}_{\ell-k}
is its
(\ell-k)
th entry
\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}
.
Hence, to cancel out the
(\ell-k)
th entry
-a_{\ell+1}
of
{\mathbf{v}}_{\ell+1}
, we proceed as:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{v}}_{\ell}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{v}}_{\ell+1}+\frac{a_{\ell+1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}{\mathbf{a}}_{\ell-k}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}-b_{\ell+1}&\cdots&-b_{\ell+1}&-b_{\ell+1}&-a_{\ell+1}&0&\cdots&0\end{bmatrix}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{a_{\ell+1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}\begin{bmatrix}-\lambda_{N,\ell}&\cdots&-\lambda_{N,\ell}&-\lambda_{N,\ell}-\lambda_{\ell,\ell-1}&\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}&0&\cdots&0\end{bmatrix}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}-b_{\ell+1}-\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}a_{\ell+1}&\cdots&-b_{\ell+1}-\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}a_{\ell+1}&-b_{\ell+1}-\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}+\lambda_{\ell,\ell-1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}a_{\ell+1}&0&0&\cdots&0\end{bmatrix}^{\intercal}.

Because
{\mathbf{v}}_{\ell}=\begin{bmatrix}-b_{\ell}&\cdots&-b_{\ell}&-a_{\ell}&0&0&\cdots&0\end{bmatrix}^{\intercal}
, we obtain the recursion

\displaystyle a_{\ell}=b_{\ell+1}+\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}+\lambda_{\ell,\ell-1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}a_{\ell+1},\quad b_{\ell}=b_{\ell+1}+\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}a_{\ell+1}\quad(\ell=N-1,\dots,k+2)

as defined in the statement of
Proposition

E.4
.
Repeating this process until we reach
\ell=k+1
, we arrive at the identity

\displaystyle{\mathbf{v}}_{k+1}={\mathbf{v}}-\lambda_{N,N-1}{\mathbf{a}}_{N-k-1}+\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-2}\frac{a_{\ell+1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}{\mathbf{a}}_{\ell-k}=0.

This implies

\displaystyle s_{N-1,k}-2\lambda_{N,k}h_{N-1,N-1}

\displaystyle=2{\mathbf{v}}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{h}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{N,N-1}\cdot 2{\mathbf{a}}_{N-k-1}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{h}}-\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-2}\frac{a_{\ell+1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}2{\mathbf{a}}_{\ell-k}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{h}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{N,N-1}\left(\frac{1}{\lambda_{N,k}}s_{k,k}-2h_{k,k}+q_{k}\right)-\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-2}\frac{a_{\ell+1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}\left(s^{\prime}_{\ell,k}-t_{\ell,k}\right)

which is the desired conclusion.
∎

Proposition E.5
.

The sequences
a_{\ell},b_{\ell}
defined in
Proposition

E.4
have the closed-form expressions

\displaystyle a_{\ell}=\frac{(N-\ell-1)!\cdot N(N-\ell+1)p_{\ell}\prod_{i=\ell}^{N-1}p_{i}}{\prod_{j=\ell+1}^{N-1}\left((N-j+1)p_{j}-1\right)},\quad b_{\ell}=\frac{(N-\ell-1)!\cdot N\prod_{i=\ell}^{N-1}p_{i}}{\prod_{j=\ell+1}^{N-1}\left((N-j+1)p_{j}-1\right)}

(73)

for
\ell=N-1,\dots,k+1
.

Proof.

We use induction on
\ell=N-1,\dots,k+1
.
In the case
\ell=N-1
,

\displaystyle b_{N-1}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{N,N-1}=Nh_{N-1,N-1}=Np_{N-1}

\displaystyle a_{N-1}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{N,N-1}+\lambda_{N-1,N-2}

\displaystyle=Nh_{N-1,N-1}+Np_{N-1}(2p_{N-1}-1)

\displaystyle=Np_{N-1}+Np_{N-1}(2p_{N-1}-1)

\displaystyle=2Np_{N-1}^{2}

which is consistent with (
73
) (with the vacuous product in the denominators being 1).
Now let
k+1\leq\ell\leq N-2
and assume that the formula (
73
) holds for
\ell+1
.
Recall that

\displaystyle\lambda_{N,\ell}

\displaystyle=\frac{N}{(N-\ell)(N-\ell-1)}-\frac{N}{N-\ell-1}p_{\ell+1}+\frac{N}{N-\ell}p_{\ell}

\displaystyle\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}

\displaystyle=\frac{N}{N-\ell-1}p_{\ell+1}\left((N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}-1\right)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\ell,\ell-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{N}{N-\ell}p_{\ell}\left((N-\ell+1)p_{\ell}-1\right).

Rewriting

\displaystyle\lambda_{N,\ell}=-\frac{N}{(N-\ell)(N-\ell-1)}\left((N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}-1\right)+\frac{N}{N-\ell}p_{\ell},

we see that

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\frac{N}{N-\ell-1}p_{\ell+1}\left((N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}-1\right)}\left(-\frac{N}{(N-\ell)(N-\ell-1)}\left((N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}-1\right)+\frac{N}{N-\ell}p_{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{(N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}}+\frac{(N-\ell-1)p_{\ell}}{(N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}\left((N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}-1\right)}.

Using the above formula and the induction hypothesis (
73
), we compute the term
b_{\ell}-b_{\ell+1}=\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}a_{\ell+1}
which comes from the recursion (
72
):

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}a_{\ell+1}

\displaystyle=\left(-\frac{1}{(N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}}+\frac{(N-\ell-1)p_{\ell}}{(N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}\left((N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}-1\right)}\right)\frac{(N-\ell-2)!\cdot N(N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}\prod_{i=\ell+1}^{N-1}p_{i}}{\prod_{j=\ell+2}^{N-1}\left((N-j+1)p_{j}-1\right)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(N-\ell-2)!\cdot N\prod_{i=\ell+1}^{N-1}p_{i}}{\prod_{j=\ell+2}^{N-1}\left((N-j+1)p_{j}-1\right)}+\frac{(N-\ell-1)!\cdot N\prod_{i=\ell}^{N-1}p_{i}}{\prod_{j=\ell+1}^{N-1}\left((N-j+1)p_{j}-1\right)}

\displaystyle=-b_{\ell+1}+\frac{(N-\ell-1)!\cdot N\prod_{i=\ell}^{N-1}p_{i}}{\prod_{j=\ell+1}^{N-1}\left((N-j+1)p_{j}-1\right)}.

This proves the identity (
73
) for
b_{\ell}
.

Similarly, we compute

\displaystyle\lambda_{N,\ell}+\lambda_{\ell,\ell-1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{N}{(N-\ell)(N-\ell-1)}\left((N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}-1\right)+\frac{N}{N-\ell}p_{\ell}+\frac{N}{N-\ell}p_{\ell}((N-\ell+1)p_{\ell}-1)

\displaystyle=-\frac{N}{(N-\ell)(N-\ell-1)}\left((N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}-1\right)+\frac{N(N-\ell+1)}{N-\ell}p_{\ell}^{2}

so

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}+\lambda_{\ell,\ell-1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\frac{N}{N-\ell-1}p_{\ell+1}\left((N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}-1\right)}\left(-\frac{N}{(N-\ell)(N-\ell-1)}\left((N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}-1\right)+\frac{N(N-\ell+1)}{N-\ell}p_{\ell}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{(N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}}+\frac{(N-\ell+1)(N-\ell-1)p_{\ell}^{2}}{(N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}\left((N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}-1\right)}.

Plugging this into the right hand side of the recursion
a_{\ell}-b_{\ell+1}=\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}+\lambda_{\ell,\ell-1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}a_{\ell+1}
we see that

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}+\lambda_{\ell,\ell-1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}a_{\ell+1}

\displaystyle=\left(-\frac{1}{(N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}}+\frac{(N-\ell+1)(N-\ell-1)p_{\ell}^{2}}{(N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}\left((N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}-1\right)}\right)\frac{(N-\ell-2)!\cdot N(N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}\prod_{i=\ell+1}^{N-1}p_{i}}{\prod_{j=\ell+2}^{N-1}\left((N-j+1)p_{j}-1\right)}

\displaystyle=-b_{\ell+1}+\frac{(N-\ell-1)!\cdot N(N-\ell+1)p_{\ell}\prod_{i=\ell}^{N-1}p_{i}}{\prod_{j=\ell+1}^{N-1}\left((N-j+1)p_{j}-1\right)}

which proves the identity (
73
) for
a_{\ell}
.
This completes the induction.
∎

From (
71
) we have

\displaystyle s_{N-1,k}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda_{N,N-1}}{\lambda_{N,k}}s_{k,k}-\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-2}\frac{a_{\ell+1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}s^{\prime}_{\ell,k}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{2\lambda_{N,k}h_{N-1,N-1}+\lambda_{N,N-1}(q_{k}-2h_{k,k})+\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-2}\frac{a_{\ell+1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}t_{\ell,k}}_{:=r_{N-1,k}}.

Because
s_{\ell,k}=0
for
\ell=k+1,\dots,N-2
, if we prove
r_{N-1,k}=0
then we are done.
Plugging the definition (
60
) of
t_{\ell,k}
’s into
r_{N-1,k}
we get

\displaystyle r_{N-1,k}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{N,k}h_{N-1,N-1}+\lambda_{N,N-1}(q_{k}-2h_{k,k})+\frac{a_{k+2}}{\lambda_{k+2,k+1}}\left(\lambda_{N,k}q_{k+1}+2\lambda_{k+1,k}(1-h_{k,k})+\lambda_{N,k+1}(q_{k}-2h_{k,k})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{\ell=k+2}^{N-2}\frac{a_{\ell+1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}\left(\lambda_{N,\ell}(q_{k}-2h_{k,k})+\lambda_{N,k}q_{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &=2\lambda_{N,k}h_{N-1,N-1}+\left(\lambda_{N,N-1}+\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-2}\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}a_{\ell+1}\right)(q_{k}-2h_{k,k})+\frac{2\lambda_{k+1,k}}{\lambda_{k+2,k+1}}a_{k+2}(1-h_{k,k})\\
&\quad\quad+\lambda_{N,k}\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-2}\frac{a_{\ell+1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}q_{\ell}.\end{aligned}

(74)

From the proof of Proposition
E.5
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}a_{\ell+1}=b_{\ell}-b_{\ell+1},

for
\ell=k+1,\dots,N-2
, so by telescoping, we can simplify:

\displaystyle\lambda_{N,N-1}+\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-2}\frac{\lambda_{N,\ell}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}a_{\ell+1}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{N,N-1}+b_{k+1}-b_{N-1}=b_{k+1}

(75)

Next, observe that

\displaystyle q_{\ell}=-((N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}-1)+(N-\ell+1)p_{\ell}

so that

\displaystyle\frac{a_{\ell+1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}q_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{1}{\frac{N}{N-\ell-1}p_{\ell+1}\left((N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}-1\right)}\left(-((N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}-1)+(N-\ell+1)p_{\ell}\right)\right]a_{\ell+1}

\displaystyle=\left[-\frac{N-\ell-1}{Np_{\ell+1}}+\frac{(N-\ell+1)(N-\ell-1)p_{\ell}}{Np_{\ell+1}((N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}-1)}\right]\frac{(N-\ell-2)!\cdot N(N-\ell)p_{\ell+1}\prod_{i=\ell+1}^{N-1}p_{i}}{\prod_{j=\ell+2}^{N-1}\left((N-j+1)p_{j}-1\right)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(N-\ell)!\prod_{i=\ell+1}^{N-1}p_{i}}{\prod_{j=\ell+2}^{N-1}\left((N-j+1)p_{j}-1\right)}+\frac{(N-\ell+1)!\prod_{i=\ell}^{N-1}p_{i}}{\prod_{j=\ell+1}^{N-1}\left((N-j+1)p_{j}-1\right)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(N-\ell)(N-\ell-1)b_{\ell+1}}{N}+\frac{(N-\ell+1)(N-\ell)b_{\ell}}{N}

which allows to simplify the summation via telescoping:

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{N-2}\frac{a_{\ell+1}}{\lambda_{\ell+1,\ell}}q_{\ell}=-2h_{N-1,N-1}+\frac{(N-k)(N-k-1)b_{k+1}}{N}

(76)

Plugging (
75
) and (
76
) into (
74
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}r_{N-1,k}&=2\lambda_{N,k}h_{N-1,N-1}+b_{k+1}(q_{k}-2h_{k,k})+\frac{2\lambda_{k+1,k}}{\lambda_{k+2,k+1}}a_{k+2}(1-h_{k,k})\\
&\quad+\lambda_{N,k}\left(-2h_{N-1,N-1}+\frac{(N-k)(N-k-1)b_{k+1}}{N}\right)\end{split}

(77)

The terms
\pm 2\lambda_{N,k}h_{N-1,N-1}
in (
77
) cancel out each other.
Now we plug in the identity

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{k+1,k}}{\lambda_{k+2,k+1}}a_{k+2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{N}{N-k-1}p_{k+1}((N-k)p_{k+1}-1)}{\frac{N}{N-k-2}p_{k+2}((N-k-1)p_{k+2}-1)}\frac{(N-k-3)!\cdot N(N-k-1)p_{k+2}\prod_{i=k+2}^{N-1}p_{i}}{\prod_{j=k+3}^{N-1}((N-j+1)p_{j}-1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{(N-k-2)!\cdot N\prod_{i=k+1}^{N-1}p_{i}}{\prod_{j=k+2}^{N-1}((N-j+1)p_{j}-1)}((N-k)p_{k+1}-1)

\displaystyle=b_{k+1}((N-k)p_{k+1}-1)

into (
77
) to obtain

\displaystyle r_{N-1,k}

\displaystyle=b_{k+1}(q_{k}-2h_{k,k})+2b_{k+1}((N-k)p_{k+1}-1)(1-h_{k,k})+\lambda_{N,k}\frac{(N-k)(N-k-1)b_{k+1}}{N}

\displaystyle=b_{k+1}\left[q_{k}-2h_{k,k}+2((N-k)p_{k+1}-1)(1-h_{k,k})+\frac{(N-k)(N-k-1)\lambda_{N,k}}{N}\right].

Finally, from the identity

\displaystyle q_{k}-2h_{k,k}+2((N-k)p_{k+1}-1)(1-h_{k,k})

\displaystyle=q_{k}-2h_{k,k}+2(N-k)p_{k+1}-2(N-k)p_{k+1}h_{k,k}-2+2h_{k,k}

\displaystyle=1-(N-k)p_{k+1}+(N-k+1)p_{k}+2(N-k)p_{k+1}-2(N-k)p_{k}-2

\displaystyle=-1+(N-k)p_{k+1}-(N-k-1)p_{k}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(N-k)(N-k-1)}{N}\lambda_{N,k}

we conclude that
r_{N-1,k}=0
.
This completes the proof of
Proposition

E.3
.

∎

Proposition

E.3
, together with
Proposition

E.2
, implies that we can inductively solve the systems
s_{\ell,k}=0
(
\ell=k,\dots,N
) from
k=N-2
to
k=1
to determine the solution
h_{i,k}^{*}
’s with
N-1\geq i\geq k
as continuous functions of
h_{1,1},\dots,h_{N-1,N-1}
.
This completes Step 4 within the proof outline of
Section

E.1
, and together with the remaining arguments from
Section

E.1
, finally proves
Theorem

4.1
.

Appendix F
Remaining proofs and details for
Section

4.2

F.1
Proof of
Proposition

4.2

For simplicity, write
\left(H_{\text{OHM}}\right)_{k,j}=h_{k,j}^{(0)}
and
\left(H_{\text{Dual-OHM}}\right)_{k,j}=h_{k,j}^{(1)}
throughout this section.
In the proof of
Theorem

4.1
in
Appendix

E
, we have seen that

\displaystyle p_{k}^{(0)}=\prod_{\ell=k}^{N-1}h_{\ell,\ell}^{(0)}=\frac{k}{N},\quad p_{k}^{(1)}=\prod_{\ell=k}^{N-1}h_{\ell,\ell}^{(1)}=\frac{1}{N-k+1}

so that

\displaystyle u:=\left(p_{2}^{(0)},\dots,p_{N-1}^{(0)}\right)\in\partial C,\quad v:=\left(p_{2}^{(1)},\dots,p_{N-1}^{(1)}\right)\in\partial C.

Recall that for
w=(p_{2},\dots,p_{N-1})\in C
,

\displaystyle\Phi(w)=\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{Np_{2}}&0&\cdots&0\\
h_{2,1}^{*}\left(\frac{1}{Np_{2}},\frac{p_{2}}{p_{3}},\dots,p_{N-1}\right)&\frac{p_{2}}{p_{3}}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
h_{N-1,1}^{*}\left(\frac{1}{Np_{2}},\frac{p_{2}}{p_{3}},\dots,p_{N-1}\right)&h_{N-1,2}^{*}\left(\frac{1}{Np_{2}},\frac{p_{2}}{p_{3}},\dots,p_{N-1}\right)&\cdots&p_{N-1}\end{bmatrix}.

Clearly, we can directly extend the definition of
\Phi
to
u,v
for diagonal entries while preserving continuity.
For non-diagonal entries, however, we have to check whether
h_{i,k}^{*}
’s with
i>k
, which are defined as solutions of linear systems, converge to
h_{i,k}^{(m)}
as
w\to\left(p_{2}^{(m)},\dots,p_{N-1}^{(m)}\right)
, for
m=0,1
.
In
Appendix

E
, we first characterize
h_{i,k}^{*}
as solutions to the set of equations
s_{\ell,k}=0
(
\ell=k,\dots,N-2
) which is equivalent to a linear system determined by the invertible lower-triangular matrix
{\mathbf{A}}
defined therein, and then show that those solutions also satisfy
s_{N-1,k}=0=s_{N,k}
.
However, within the process of rewriting the system
s_{\ell,k}=0
(
\ell=k,\dots,N-2
) in terms of
{\mathbf{A}}
we use division by
\lambda_{N,k}
(so we are implicitly assuming
\lambda_{N,k}>0
), and the resulting matrix
{\mathbf{A}}
is not invertible if one of
\lambda_{j+1,j}
(
j=k+1,\dots,N-2
) is zero.
Because
\lambda_{N,k}=0
(
k=1,\dots,N-2
) for
OHM
and
\lambda_{j+1,j}=0
(
j=1,\dots,N-2
) for
Dual-OHM
, the same argument is not directly applicable to these cases.

Instead, we retreat to the original set of equations and consider the system
s_{\ell,k}=0
(
k=k+1,\dots,N-1
), which has the matrix form

\displaystyle\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{k+2,k+1}+\lambda_{N,k+1}&\lambda_{N,k+1}&\cdots&\lambda_{N,k+1}&\lambda_{N,k+1}\\
-\lambda_{k+2,k+1}&\lambda_{k+3,k+2}+\lambda_{N,k+2}&\cdots&\lambda_{N,k+2}&\lambda_{N,k+2}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&\lambda_{N-1,N-2}+\lambda_{N,N-2}&\lambda_{N,N-2}\\
0&0&\cdots&-\lambda_{N-1,N-2}&\lambda_{N,N-1}\end{bmatrix}}_{:={\mathbf{B}}}\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}h_{k+1,k}\\
h_{k+2,k}\\
\vdots\\
h_{N-2,k}\\
h_{N-1,k}\end{bmatrix}}_{{\mathbf{h}}}\quad\quad\quad\quad

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\begin{bmatrix}-2\lambda_{k+1,k}(1-h_{k,k})-\lambda_{N,k}(q_{k}-2h_{k,k})\\
-\lambda_{N,k}q_{k+2}\\
\vdots\\
-\lambda_{N,k}q_{N-2}\\
-2\lambda_{N,k}h_{N-1,N-1}\end{bmatrix}}_{:={\mathbf{b}}}.

As we take
w\to u
(
u
is the
(p_{2},\dots,p_{N-1})
-coordinate for
OHM
), all
\lambda_{N,j}
with
j=k+1,\dots,N-2
vanishes, and
{\mathbf{B}}
converges to the lower bidiagonal matrix

\displaystyle{\mathbf{B}}^{(0)}=\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{k+2,k+1}^{(0)}&&&\\
-\lambda_{k+2,k+1}^{(0)}&\lambda_{k+3,k+2}^{(0)}&&\\
&\ddots&\ddots&\\
&&-\lambda_{N-1,N-2}^{(0)}&\lambda_{N,N-1}^{(0)}\end{bmatrix}

where
\lambda_{j+1,j}^{(0)}=\frac{j(j+1)}{N}
denotes the limit of
\lambda_{j+1,j}
as
w\to u
(up to a constant, these are the weights multiplied to the monotonicity inequalities in the convergence proof of
OHM
).
It is clear that
{\mathbf{B}}^{(0)}
is invertible, and because
\lambda
’s depend continuously on
p_{2},\dots,p_{N-1}
, the matrix
{\mathbf{B}}
is invertible in a neighborhood of
u
.
Within this neighborhood, the solutions
h_{k+1,k}^{*},\dots,h_{N-1,k}^{*}
are determined via Cramer’s rule applied to the system
{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{h}}={\mathbf{b}}
.
As all entries of
{\mathbf{b}}
depend continuously on
p_{2},\dots,p_{N-1}
(because
\lambda
’s and
q_{j}
’s are), this proves that
h_{i,k}^{*}\to h_{i,k}^{(0)}
as
w\to u
for each
i=k+1,\dots,N-1
.
(Note that
h_{i,k}^{(0)}
must be the unique solutions satisfying
{\mathbf{B}}^{(0)}{\mathbf{h}}={\mathbf{b}}^{(0)}=\lim_{w\to u}{\mathbf{b}}
, as
OHM
also satisfies the identity (
41
).)

Similarly, for the case of
Dual-OHM
, we observe that all
\lambda_{j+1,j}
with
j=k+1,\dots,N-2
vanish in the limit
w\to v
, so
{\mathbf{B}}
converges to the upper triangular matrix

\displaystyle{\mathbf{B}}^{(1)}=\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{N,k+1}^{(1)}&\lambda_{N,k+1}^{(1)}&\cdots&\lambda_{N,k+1}^{(1)}\\
&\lambda_{N,k+2}^{(1)}&\cdots&\lambda_{N,k+2}^{(1)}\\
&&\ddots&\vdots&\\
&&&\lambda_{N,N-1}^{(1)}\end{bmatrix}

where
\lambda_{N,j}^{(1)}=\frac{N}{(N-j)(N-j+1)}=\lim_{w\to v}\lambda_{N,j}
(as before, these are the weights multiplied to the monotonicity inequalities in the convergence proof of
Dual-OHM
up to a constant).
Again,
{\mathbf{B}}^{(1)}
is invertible and thus
h_{i,k}^{*}
are determined via Cramer’s rule applied to the system
{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{h}}={\mathbf{b}}
in a neighborhood of
v
, which shows that
h_{i,k}^{*}\to h_{i,k}^{(1)}
as
w\to v
.

The above arguments altogether show that

\begin{array}[]{lc}\Phi(w)\to H_{\text{OHM}}&\text{as }\,\,w\to u\\
\Phi(w)\to H_{\text{Dual-OHM}}&\text{as }\,\,w\to v\\
\end{array}

and thus concludes the proof of
Proposition

4.2
.

F.2
Explicit characterization of optimal algorithm family for the case
N=3

For a nonexpansive operator
{\mathbb{T}}
, we let
{\mathbb{A}}=2({\mathbb{I}}+{\mathbb{T}})^{-1}-{\mathbb{I}}
,
x_{k+1}={\mathbb{J}}_{{\mathbb{A}}}(y_{k})
and
\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+1}=y_{k}-x_{k+1}
as usual.
Consider the algorithm defined by

\displaystyle y_{1}

\displaystyle=y_{0}-h_{1,1}(y_{0}-{\mathbb{T}}y_{0})

\displaystyle=y_{0}-2h_{1,1}\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1}

\displaystyle y_{2}

\displaystyle=y_{1}-h_{2,1}(y_{0}-{\mathbb{T}}y_{0})-h_{2,2}(y_{1}-{\mathbb{T}}y_{1})

\displaystyle=y_{1}-2h_{2,1}\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1}-2h_{2,2}\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{2}

where
h_{1,1},h_{2,2}\in\left[\frac{1}{2},\frac{2}{3}\right]
,
h_{1,1}h_{2,2}=\frac{1}{3}
and
h_{1,1}+h_{2,1}+h_{2,2}=1
.
Let

\displaystyle\lambda_{3,2}=3h_{2,2},\quad\lambda_{3,1}=2-3h_{2,2},\quad\lambda_{2,1}=3h_{2,2}(2h_{2,2}-1)

as defined in (
52
) (we substitute
N=3
and
p_{2}=h_{2,2}
).
Clearly, we have
\lambda_{3,2},\lambda_{3,1},\lambda_{2,1}\geq 0
because
\frac{1}{2}\leq h_{2,2}\leq\frac{2}{3}
.

Now, let
g_{k}=\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k}
for
k=1,2,3
.
Then

\displaystyle x_{2}-x_{1}

\displaystyle=(y_{1}-g_{2})-(y_{0}-g_{1})=(y_{1}-y_{0})-(g_{2}-g_{1})=(1-2h_{1,1})g_{1}-g_{2}

\displaystyle x_{3}-x_{2}

\displaystyle=(y_{2}-g_{3})-(y_{1}-g_{2})=(y_{2}-y_{1})-(g_{3}-g_{2})=-2h_{2,1}g_{1}+(1-2h_{2,2})g_{2}-g_{3}

\displaystyle x_{3}-x_{1}

\displaystyle=(x_{3}-x_{2})+(x_{2}-x_{1})=(1-2h_{1,1}-2h_{2,1})g_{1}-2h_{2,2}g_{2}-g_{3}

\displaystyle x_{3}-y_{0}

\displaystyle=(x_{3}-x_{1})-(y_{0}-x_{1})=x_{3}-x_{1}-g_{1}=-(2h_{1,1}+2h_{2,1})g_{1}-2h_{2,2}g_{2}-g_{3}

so we can expand

\displaystyle\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{3},x_{3}-y_{0}\right\rangle+3\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{3}\right\|^{2}+\lambda_{2,1}\left\langle x_{2}-x_{1},\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{2}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1}\right\rangle+\lambda_{3,1}\left\langle x_{3}-x_{1},\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{3}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1}\right\rangle+\lambda_{3,2}\left\langle x_{3}-x_{2},\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{3}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle g_{3},x_{3}-y_{0}\right\rangle+3\left\|g_{3}\right\|^{2}+\lambda_{2,1}\left\langle x_{2}-x_{1},g_{2}-g_{1}\right\rangle+\lambda_{3,1}\left\langle x_{3}-x_{1},g_{3}-g_{1}\right\rangle+\lambda_{3,2}\left\langle x_{3}-x_{2},g_{3}-g_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle g_{3},-(2h_{1,1}+2h_{2,1})g_{1}-2h_{2,2}g_{2}-g_{3}\right\rangle+3\left\|g_{3}\right\|^{2}+\lambda_{2,1}\left\langle(1-2h_{1,1})g_{1}-g_{2},g_{2}-g_{1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\lambda_{3,1}\left\langle(1-2h_{1,1}-2h_{2,1})g_{1}-2h_{2,2}g_{2}-g_{3},g_{3}-g_{1}\right\rangle+\lambda_{3,2}\left\langle-2h_{2,1}g_{1}+(1-2h_{2,2})g_{2}-g_{3},g_{3}-g_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left(\lambda_{2,1}(2h_{1,1}-1)-\lambda_{3,1}(1-2h_{1,1}-2h_{2,1})\right)\left\|g_{1}\right\|^{2}+\left(\lambda_{2,1}(2-2h_{1,1})+2\lambda_{3,1}h_{2,2}+2\lambda_{3,2}h_{2,1}\right)\left\langle g_{2},g_{1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(-(2h_{1,1}+2h_{2,1})+\lambda_{3,1}(2-2h_{1,1}-2h_{2,1})-2\lambda_{3,2}h_{2,1}\right)\left\langle g_{3},g_{1}\right\rangle+\left(-\lambda_{2,1}+\lambda_{3,2}(2h_{2,2}-1)\right)\left\|g_{2}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(-2h_{2,2}-2\lambda_{3,1}h_{2,2}+\lambda_{3,2}(2-2h_{2,2})\right)\left\langle g_{3},g_{2}\right\rangle+\left(-1+3-\lambda_{3,1}-\lambda_{3,2}\right)\left\|g_{3}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=s_{1,1}\left\|g_{1}\right\|^{2}+s_{2,1}\left\langle g_{2},g_{1}\right\rangle+s_{3,1}\left\langle g_{3},g_{1}\right\rangle+s_{2,2}\left\|g_{2}\right\|^{2}+s_{3,2}\left\langle g_{3},g_{2}\right\rangle+s_{3,3}\left\|g_{3}\right\|^{2}.

We verify:

\displaystyle s_{3,3}

\displaystyle=2-\lambda_{3,1}-\lambda_{3,2}=0

\displaystyle s_{3,2}

\displaystyle=-2h_{2,2}-2\lambda_{3,1}h_{2,2}+\lambda_{3,2}(2-2h_{2,2})

\displaystyle=-2h_{2,2}-2(2-3h_{2,2})h_{2,2}+3h_{2,2}(2-2h_{2,2})=0

\displaystyle s_{2,2}

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{2,1}+\lambda_{3,2}(2h_{2,2}-1)

\displaystyle=-3h_{2,2}(2h_{2,2}-1)+3h_{2,2}(2h_{2,2}-1)=0.

Next,

\displaystyle s_{3,1}

\displaystyle=-(2h_{1,1}+2h_{2,1})+\lambda_{3,1}(2-2h_{1,1}-2h_{2,1})-2\lambda_{3,2}h_{2,1}

\displaystyle=2(h_{2,2}-1)+(2-3h_{2,2})2h_{2,2}-6h_{2,2}\left(1-\frac{1}{3h_{2,2}}-h_{2,2}\right)=0

where we plug in the definitions of
\lambda_{3,2}
and eliminate
h_{2,1},h_{1,1}
using the conditions
h_{1,1}h_{2,2}=\frac{1}{3}
and
h_{1,1}+h_{2,1}+h_{2,2}=1
to obtain the second equality.
Similarly,

\displaystyle s_{2,1}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{2,1}(2-2h_{1,1})+2\lambda_{3,1}h_{2,2}+2\lambda_{3,2}h_{2,1}

\displaystyle=3h_{2,2}(2h_{2,2}-1)\left(2-\frac{2}{3h_{2,2}}\right)+2(2-3h_{2,2})h_{2,2}+6h_{2,2}\left(1-\frac{1}{3h_{2,2}}-h_{2,2}\right)

\displaystyle=(2h_{2,2}-1)(6h_{2,2}-2)+2h_{2,2}(2-3h_{2,2})+6h_{2,2}-2-6h_{2,2}^{2}

\displaystyle=0

and

\displaystyle s_{1,1}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{2,1}(2h_{1,1}-1)-\lambda_{3,1}(1-2h_{1,1}-2h_{2,1})

\displaystyle=3h_{2,2}(2h_{2,2}-1)\left(\frac{2}{3h_{2,2}}-1\right)-(2-3h_{2,2})(2h_{2,2}-1)

\displaystyle=(2h_{2,2}-1)(2-3h_{2,2})-(2-3h_{2,2})(2h_{2,2}-1)

\displaystyle=0.

This shows that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{3},x_{3}-y_{0}\right\rangle+3\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{3}\right\|^{2}+\lambda_{2,1}\left\langle x_{2}-x_{1},\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{2}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\lambda_{3,1}\left\langle x_{3}-x_{1},\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{3}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1}\right\rangle+\lambda_{3,2}\left\langle x_{3}-x_{2},\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{3}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{3},x_{3}-y_{0}\right\rangle+3\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{3}\right\|^{2},

which, together with
Lemma

3.2
, proves
\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{3}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{\left\|y_{0}-y_{\star}\right\|^{2}}{9}
.

Note that our choice of
h_{2,1}
is consistent with the formula (
53
) for
k=1
, i.e.,
2(h_{1,1}+h_{2,1})=2-2h_{2,2}=1-2p_{2}+3p_{1}
.
Indeed, the calculation above is just a concrete demonstration of the more complicated series of computations presented in
Appendix

E
.

F.3
Examples of optimal algorithms not covered by
Theorem

4.1

Let
N>3
, and fix
2\leq N^{\prime}\leq N-1
.
Consider the following algorithm that first runs
N^{\prime}
steps of
Dual-OHM
(with
y_{N^{\prime}-1}
as terminal iterate) and then
N-N^{\prime}
steps of
OHM
(as if
y_{N^{\prime}-1}
was generated by usual
OHM
and then continuing on using the update rule of
OHM
):

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} y_{k+1}&=y_{k}+\frac{N^{\prime}-k-1}{N^{\prime}-k}({\mathbb{T}}y_{k}-{\mathbb{T}}y_{k-1})\quad&&\text{if }\,\,k=0,\dots,N^{\prime}-2\\
y_{k+1}&=\frac{k+1}{k+2}{\mathbb{T}}y_{k}+\frac{1}{k+2}y_{0}\quad&&\text{if }\,\,k=N^{\prime}-1,\dots,N-2.\end{aligned}

(78)

We show that this algorithm also has the exact optimal rate

\displaystyle\left\|y_{N-1}-{\mathbb{T}}y_{N-1}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{4\left\|y_{0}-y_{\star}\right\|^{2}}{N^{2}}.

First, because
y_{N^{\prime}-1}
is generated via Dual-OHM for
N^{\prime}-1
iterations, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N^{\prime}},x_{N^{\prime}}-y_{0}\right\rangle+N^{\prime}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N^{\prime}}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N^{\prime}},x_{N^{\prime}}-y_{0}\right\rangle+N^{\prime}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N^{\prime}}\right\|^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{N^{\prime}-1}\frac{N^{\prime}}{(N^{\prime}-j)(N^{\prime}-j+1)}\left\langle x_{N^{\prime}}-x_{j},\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N^{\prime}}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=0

where as usual,
{\mathbb{A}}=2({\mathbb{I}}+{\mathbb{T}})^{-1}-{\mathbb{I}}
,
x_{k+1}={\mathbb{J}}_{{\mathbb{A}}}(y_{k})
and
\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+1}=y_{k}-x_{k+1}
.
Now from the analysis of OHM, the identity

\displaystyle\underbrace{\left((k+1)^{2}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+1}\right\|^{2}+(k+1)\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+1},x_{k+1}-y_{0}\right\rangle\right)}_{U_{k+1}}-\underbrace{\left((k+2)^{2}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+2}\right\|^{2}+(k+2)\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+2},x_{k+2}-y_{0}\right\rangle\right)}_{U_{k+2}}

\displaystyle=(k+1)(k+2)\left\langle x_{k+2}-x_{k+1},\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}{x_{k+2}-x_{k+1}}\right\rangle

holds if the relationship

\displaystyle y_{k+1}=\frac{k+1}{k+2}{\mathbb{T}}y_{k}+\frac{1}{k+2}y_{0}=\frac{k+1}{k+2}(y_{k}-2\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{k+1})+\frac{1}{k+2}y_{0}

holds (regardless of whether
y_{k}
is an iterate generated by OHM or not); see, e.g.,
Ryu & Yin (2022)
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle N^{2}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}+N\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N},x_{N}-y_{0}\right\rangle=U_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\leq U_{N-1}\leq\cdots\leq U_{N^{\prime}}=(N^{\prime})^{2}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N^{\prime}}\right\|^{2}+N^{\prime}\left\langle\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N^{\prime}},x_{N^{\prime}}-y_{0}\right\rangle\leq 0

and dividing both sides by
N
and applying
Lemma

3.2
we obtain
\left\|\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{\left\|y_{0}-y_{\star}\right\|^{2}}{N^{2}}
.

However, one can expect that the algorithm (
78
) will not belong to the optimal algorithm family in
Theorem

4.1
, because its convergence proof above uses the set of inequalities

\displaystyle\lambda_{i,j}\left\langle x_{i}-x_{j},\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{i}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{j}\right\rangle

with
(i,j)\in\{(N^{\prime},1),\dots,(N^{\prime},N^{\prime}-1)\}\cup\{(N^{\prime}+1,N^{\prime}),\dots,(N,N-1)\}
, which is different from the set
\{(N,1),\dots,(N,N-1)\}\cup\{(2,1),\dots,(N,N-1)\}
used in
Theorem

4.1
.
Indeed, consider the algorithm (
78
) in the case
N^{\prime}=N-1
.
Then the upper
(N-2)\times(N-2)
submatrix of its H-matrix equals the H-matrix of
Dual-OHM
for with terminal iterate
y_{N-2}
.
Next, it can be checked that the sum of each column in the H-matrix of
Dual-OHM
is always
\frac{1}{2}
(regardless of the total iteration count), so that
y_{N-2}=y_{0}-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1}-\cdots-\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N-2}
.
Then we see that

\displaystyle y_{N-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{N-1}{N}{\mathbb{T}}y_{N-2}+\frac{1}{N}y_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{N-1}{N}(y_{N-2}-2\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N-1})+\frac{1}{N}y_{0}

\displaystyle=y_{0}-\frac{N-1}{N}\left(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1}+\cdots+\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N-2}\right)-\frac{2(N-1)}{N}\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N-1}

\displaystyle=y_{N-2}+\frac{1}{N}\left(\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{1}+\cdots+\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N-2}\right)-\frac{2(N-1)}{N}\tilde{{\mathbb{A}}}x_{N-1},

and thus, the H-matrix of (
78
) with
N^{\prime}=N-1
is

\displaystyle H=\begin{bmatrix}\frac{N-2}{N-1}&&&\\
\vdots&\ddots&&\\
-\frac{1}{(N-1)(N-2)}&\cdots&\frac{1}{2}\\
-\frac{1}{2N}&\cdots&-\frac{1}{2N}&\frac{N-1}{N}\end{bmatrix}.

For this H-matrix, we have

\displaystyle p_{N-1}=\frac{N-1}{N},\quad p_{N-2}=\frac{N-1}{2N},\quad\dots,\quad p_{2}=\frac{N-1}{(N-2)N}

and it can be checked that
w=(p_{2},\dots,p_{N-1})\in C
.
However, if
H=\Phi(w)
, then by construction of
\Phi
(see (
55
), (
56
)) we must have

\displaystyle 2(h_{N-1,N-2}+h_{N-2,N-2})=1-2p_{N-1}+3p_{N-2}.

However, the left hand side is

\displaystyle 2\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2N}\right)=\frac{N-1}{N},

while the right hand side is

\displaystyle 1-\frac{2(N-1)}{N}+\frac{3(N-1)}{2N}=\frac{N+1}{2N},

which is a contradiction.
Therefore, we conclude that
H\neq\Phi(w)
.

Appendix G
Omitted details from
Section

6

G.1
Lyapunov analysis of
Dual-FEG

In this section, we denote
{\mathbb{A}}x=\nabla_{\pm}\mathbf{L}(x)
for simplicity.
Recall the update rules of
Dual-FEG
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}x_{k+1/2}&=x_{k}-\alpha z_{k}-\alpha{\mathbb{A}}{x_{k}}\\
x_{k+1}&=x_{k+1/2}-\frac{N-k-1}{N-k}\alpha\left({\mathbb{A}}x_{k+1/2}-{\mathbb{A}}x_{k}\right)\\
z_{k+1}&=\frac{N-k-1}{N-k}z_{k}-\frac{1}{N-k}{\mathbb{A}}{x_{k+1/2}},\end{split}

(Dual-FEG)

and the Lyapunov function

\displaystyle V_{k}=-\alpha\left\|z_{k}+{\mathbb{A}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}+\frac{2}{N-k}\left\langle z_{k}+{\mathbb{A}}x_{N},x_{k}-x_{N}\right\rangle

for
k=1,\dots,N-1
.
We prove that

\displaystyle V_{k}-V_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{(N-k)(N-k-1)}\underbrace{\left\langle{\mathbb{A}}x_{N}-{\mathbb{A}}x_{k+1/2},x_{N}-x_{k+1/2}\right\rangle}_{\mathrm{MI}_{k}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\alpha(N-k)^{2}}\underbrace{\Big(\|x_{k+1/2}-x_{k}\|^{2}-\alpha^{2}\|{\mathbb{A}}x_{k+1/2}-{\mathbb{A}}x_{k}\|^{2}\Big)}_{\mathrm{LI}_{k}}

for
k=1,\dots,N-2
.
This implies
V_{k}\geq V_{k+1}
because
\mathrm{MI}_{k}\geq 0
by monotonicity of
{\mathbb{A}}
and
\mathrm{LI}_{k}\geq 0
from
L
-Lipschitz continuity of
{\mathbb{A}}
(
L
-smoothness of
{\mathbf{L}}
) and
0<\alpha\leq\frac{1}{L}
.

We first decompose
V_{k}
as following:

\displaystyle V_{k}=-\alpha\left\|z_{k}\right\|^{2}-\alpha\left\|{\mathbb{A}}x_{N}\right\|^{2}+\underbrace{\left(\frac{2}{N-k}\left\langle{\mathbb{A}}x_{N},x_{k}-x_{N}\right\rangle-2\alpha\left\langle{\mathbb{A}}x_{N},z_{k}\right\rangle\right)}_{:=V_{k}^{(1)}}+\underbrace{\frac{2}{N-k}\left\langle z_{k},x_{k}-x_{N}\right\rangle}_{:=V_{k}^{(2)}}.

Observe that
x_{k+1/2}
can be written in the following two ways:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} x_{k+1/2}&=x_{k}-\alpha z_{k}-\alpha{\mathbb{A}}{x_{k}}\\
x_{k+1/2}&=x_{k+1}-\frac{N-k-1}{N-k}\alpha({\mathbb{A}}x_{k}-{\mathbb{A}}x_{k+1/2}).\end{aligned}

(79)

Appropriately plugging (
79
) into
\mathrm{MI}_{k}
we can rewrite it as

\displaystyle\mathrm{MI}_{k}

\displaystyle=(N-k)\left\langle{\mathbb{A}}x_{N},x_{N}-x_{k+1/2}\right\rangle-(N-k-1)\left\langle{\mathbb{A}}x_{N},x_{N}-x_{k+1/2}\right\rangle-\left\langle{\mathbb{A}}x_{k+1/2},x_{N}-x_{k+1/2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=(N-k)\left\langle{\mathbb{A}}x_{N},x_{N}-x_{k+1}+\frac{N-k-1}{N-k}\alpha({\mathbb{A}}x_{k}-{\mathbb{A}}x_{k+1/2})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad-(N-k-1)\left\langle{\mathbb{A}}x_{N},x_{N}-x_{k}+\alpha z_{k}+\alpha{\mathbb{A}}x_{k}\right\rangle-\left\langle{\mathbb{A}}x_{k+1/2},x_{N}-x_{k+1/2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=(N-k)\left\langle{\mathbb{A}}x_{N},x_{N}-x_{k+1}\right\rangle-(N-k-1)\left\langle{\mathbb{A}}x_{N},x_{N}-x_{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\alpha(N-k-1)\left\langle{\mathbb{A}}x_{N},{\mathbb{A}}x_{k+1/2}+z_{k}\right\rangle-\left\langle{\mathbb{A}}x_{k+1/2},x_{N}-x_{k+1/2}\right\rangle.

Now multiply
\frac{2}{(N-k)(N-k-1)}
to
\mathrm{MI}_{k}
:

\displaystyle\frac{2}{(N-k)(N-k-1)}\mathrm{MI}_{k}
